State Programmatic Snapshots
State Snapshot: 2026 Release

State Programmatic Snapshots
The Rural Health Transformation (RHT) Program helps State governments to support rural communities across America in improvin g healthcare 
access, quality, and outcomes by transforming the healthcare delivery ecosystem. These State Program Snapshots summarize Stat e RHT Program 
initiatives and their focus areas as well as State Policy Action Commitments tied to technical score factors. This informatio n was drawn from State 
RHT Program applications and Annual Reports. States had substantial flexibility in determining the number and content of thei r initiatives.
The State Policy Actions shown on each slide reflect the policies a State had in place at the time of application and commitm ents it made to 
further progress. If a State did not have an existing policy at baseline and did not make a commitment to progress at the tim e of application, the 
State Policy Action was omitted from the slide. States may have associated funding listed for State Policy Actions with "N/A" status, which 
indicates that the State already had a baseline policy in place for that State Policy Action.
The Total State Policy Action Commitments number is the sum of all commitments made across each policy factor. For example, i f a State 
committed to joining multiple licensure compacts, each compact commitment is counted individually. Additionally, some score f actors, such as 
C.3 Certificate of Need or D.3 Scope of Practice, allow States to commit to highly specific sub -factors (e.g., Scope of Practice Dental Hygienist –
Local Anesthesia). These sub -factors are listed in the Status column. However, if a State has multiple sub -factors within the sa me category, they 
are collectively counted only one time toward the overall Total State Policy Action Commitments number (i.e., each individual sub-factor in the 
same category is not counted separately). Therefore, the sum of the individual entries in the Status column may be different than the Total State 
Policy Action Commitments shown at the top of the slide.

State Programmatic Snapshots
Each State’s funding for Budget Period 1 and 2 of the Program is noted, based on Notices of Award that were released in Decem ber 2025 and 
October 2026, respectively. Award funding amounts are presented in millions and are rounded. For Budget Periods 1 and 2, Stat es received equal 
shares of baseline funding as well as a variable amount of workload funding tied to rural demographics and technical scores ( including baseline 
policies for State Policy Actions). CMS recalculates technical scores annually. 
For Budget Period 1, States received slightly higher funding across all score factors, except B.4. The B.4 Physician Nutritio n Continuing Medical 
Education State Policy Action was not scored during Budget Period 1; therefore, the funding attributed to B.4 was redistribut ed across the 
remaining score factors for Budget Period 1. For Budget Period 2, CMS recalculated each State’s technical score, adjusting ea ch State’s workload 
funding based on progress toward initiative checkpoints, progress on State Policy Action Commitments, and changes in the data -driven technical 
score factors. The rural demographic score factors remain static across budget periods. 

Alabama
State Snapshot: 2026 Release

Alabama $199.28M
Budget Period 2 
Funding
Initiative Name Initiative Description
Collaborative EHR, 
Information Technology (IT), 
and Cybersecurity
Modernizes healthcare IT infrastructure by establishing regional hubs for IT and cybersecurity support and will assist with EHR integration 
for rural hospitals and clinics. Reduces overall technology costs and improve interoperability while allowing independent providers to 
maintain their organizational autonomy.
Rural Health
Expands access to specialty and emergent care by creating a coordinated network of telehealth hubs. Links first responders, EMS, and 
local hospitals directly to regional referral centers. Delivers advanced services like remote patient monitoring, tele-stroke, and tele-
behavioral health directly into rural communities to reduce unnecessary patient transfers.
Maternal and Fetal Health
Connects rural facilities with maternal-fetal medicine specialists through advanced digital tools and provides access to telerobotic 
ultrasound. Addresses critical shortages of local obstetric services to reduce high-risk maternal and infant mortality. Expands an 
emergency labor and delivery cart pilot program to offer lifesaving stabilization for newborns and mothers pending transfer to a more 
appropriate hospital setting.
Rural Workforce
Invests in coordinated training and recruitment pipelines for a broad range of clinical healthcare professionals including but not limited to, 
physicians, nurses, EMS professionals, certified nurse midwives, dentists, dental hygienists, and dental assistants. Grows the local 
workforce by leveraging remote learning and strategic clinical partnerships with educational institutions. Provides financialincentives for 
providers to practice in rural areas while expanding Graduate Medical Education (GME) programs.
Cancer Digital 
Regionalization
Replicates the successful, evidence-based model of Alabama's Operation Wipe Out program to target various cancers for early detection. 
Utilizes decentralized, cross-sector partnerships where local organizations lead screening efforts in their own communities. Offers an 
innovative approach to preventive screening that achieves greater efficiency and broader reach than traditional clinics.
Simulation Training
Improves the quality of care in rural healthcare facilities by providing advanced training in specialized medical services. Enhances 
workforce capabilities and increases patient volumes for local providers while reducing required travel times. Ensures patients receive 
appropriate treatment close to home to successfully reduce overcrowding at larger tertiary care hospitals. A goal of this initiative is to 
increase the amount of simulation training offered each year and to create and implement additional programs in other specialties. 
$203.40M
Budget Period 1 
Funding

Initiative Name Initiative Description
Statewide Emergency 
Medical Service (EMS) 
Trauma and Stroke
Expands statewide hospital-referral services to route ambulances to the most appropriate available facilities for complex conditions. 
Addresses acute patient needs such as psychiatric distress, labor and delivery, and severe respiratory issues. Secures critical funding to 
support the lifesaving expansion of the existing Alabama Trauma Communications Center, the only EMS agency with a statewide reach. 
The Center directs ambulances statewide to roughly 100 urban and rural hospitals across Alabama and in four neighboring states.
EMS Treat-In-Place
Implements an on-site treatment model that model would allow EMS providers to treat patients and be reimbursed for their services, 
saving  transportation costs, reducing Emergency Department overcrowding, and decreasing the resource burden on hospitals. This 
initiative also creates a needed revenue source for EMS providers and keeps ambulances in the community, thereby reducing response 
times and improving overall care for residents, especially in rural areas. 
Mental Health
Aims to expand mental health access for a school-based tele-mental health model and conversion of Community Mental Health Centers 
(CMHCs) to Certified Community Behavioral Health Clinics (CCBHCs). The first phase will increase awareness to educators, students, 
and their families while providing the broadest level of access to appropriate services and will help address Alabama’s urgent youth 
mental health needs, as 1 in 6 Alabama youth experience a mental health disorder. The second phase will allow the remaining CMHCs the 
opportunity to apply for funding to convert to a CCBHC to provide patients with coordinated comprehensive behavioral health care for 
mental health or substance use disorders.
Community Medicine
Serves rural communities by providing free preventive health screenings and navigating connections to appropriate medical care. 
Addresses regional food access issues and promotes physical fitness alongside healthy lifestyle education. This initiative will expand 
access to health and nutrition services; create new mobile service delivery models that can operate across multiple regions; reduce 
health disparities in rural and underserved communities; improve early detection, chronic disease management, and nutrition-related 
outcomes; and strengthen the State’s long-term public health infrastructure.
Rural Health Practice
Establishes and expands networked clinics in underserved areas to serve as comprehensive, patient-centered hubs. Emphasizes 
preventive care and chronic disease management by combining physical and behavioral health services through telehealth. Offers local 
facilities opportunities to partner with schools to provide primary care and dental services to students and families.

B.3. SNAP 
Waivers
SNAP provides food benefits to low -income families to supplement their 
grocery budget. Restricting the use of SNAP benefits on non -nutritious foods 
can help improve dietary intake and clinical indicators associated with long -
term disease in rural populations. This State Policy Action captures State 
efforts to obtain a USDA SNAP Food Restriction Waiver to prohibit 
purchase of non-nutritious items.
At baseline, AL had active 
legislation to create 
SNAP waiver
AL committed to 
this State Policy 
Action In Progress $4.98M
C.3. Certificate 
of Need
Certificate of Need (CON) laws require healthcare providers to seek 
permission from the State before they can open new facilities or expand 
service offerings. Eliminating or loosening CON laws allows providers to 
establish new facilities in underserved rural regions without increased 
burden, addressing the scarcity of local care options. This State Policy 
Action captures State efforts to ease CON laws across several service 
and facility types.
At baseline, AL had 
stringent CON 
regulations
No State

Alaska
State Snapshot: 2026 Release

Alaska $272.65M
Budget Period 2 
Funding
Initiative Name Initiative Description
Healthy Beginnings
Strengthens Alaska's maternal, infant, and early childhood health system as a foundation for healthy families, particularly in rural and 
frontier areas. Funds will expand access to prenatal and postpartum care closer to home, strengthen family-centered home visiting and 
early intervention services, and build healthy lifestyle programs that promote prevention and resilience from birth through adolescence. 
Health Care Access
Strengthens primary, behavioral, oral, specialty, emergency, long-term, and post-acute care by: expanding integrated 
primary/behavioral/oral health; improving specialty access via telehealth, remote monitoring, and mobile teams; bolstering rural hospital 
capacity for 24/7 urgent, emergency, labor & delivery, and trauma care; supporting seniors and people with intellectual and 
developmental disabilities (IDD) to access aging-in-place and home- and community-based services; and improving post-acute and 
recovery care at home or in the community.
Healthy Communities
Advances prevention and chronic disease management across Alaska’s rural, remote, and frontier regions. It strengthens locally tailored 
primary and preventive care, expands digital tools and population health infrastructure for early intervention, and promotes healthy 
lifestyles through nutrition, physical activity, and community wellness programs. 
Pay for Value
Supports Alaska's transition from fee-for-service to sustainable, value-based payment models that reward quality, coordination, and 
prevention. It builds the financial and operational foundation rural providers need to thrive under new reimbursement approaches by 
testing primary care and hospital payment models, evaluating readiness for the Center for Medicare and Medicaid Innovation (CMMI) 
Achieving Healthcare Efficiency through Accountable Design (AHEAD) Model, and creating alternative payment structures for integrated, 
high-value care. 
$272.17M
Budget Period 1 
Funding

Alaska
Initiative Name Initiative Description
Strengthen Workforce
Builds a sustainable and resilient rural health care workforce by expanding local education and training pipelines, supporting 
recruitment and retention, and strengthening wraparound supports for health professionals and trainees. Funds will develop “grow our 
own” pathways, expand training and certification for clinical and community providers, enhance residencies and rotations, andprovide 
housing, childcare, and incentive programs that help attract and retain qualified providers in Alaska's rural, remote, and frontier 
communities. 
Spark T echnology and 
Innovation
Expands telehealth, remote monitoring, and artificial intelligence (AI)-enabled tools; replaces outdated systems with  Health Information 
Technology for Economic and Clinical Health (HITECH)-certified electronic medical records; and increases participation in the statewide 
Health Information Exchange (HIE). The initiative supports community navigation and self-management tools, enhances cybersecurity, 
and pilots technologies such as drones, kiosks, and remote dispensing to improve care in remote areas. Training and technical
assistance (TA) will help providers adopt new tools and workflows, while public/private partnerships and state-directed targeted 
innovation awards will accelerate scalable, sustainable technology solutions that advance interoperability and long-term 
transformation.

Arizona
State Snapshot: 2026 Release

Arizona $165.01M
Budget Period 2 
Funding
Initiative Name Initiative Description
Rural Health Workforce 
Development and Training 
Program
Deploys a comprehensive, multi-pronged strategy to recruit, train, and retain skilled health professionals in rural areas. Prioritizes non-
physician providers such as allied health professionals, community health workers, nurses, and physician assistants. Invests 
subsequently in the primary and specialty care physician workforce to meet urgent community needs.
Priority Health Initiatives 
Grants Portfolio 
Advances health outcomes across Arizona by funding high-impact, community-driven programs for screening and preventive care. Targets 
key areas including behavioral health, chronic disease prevention, and maternal-fetal health disparities. Partners with local organizations 
to deliver these critical resources directly to the communities that need them most.
Making Rural Healthcare 
Accessible
Expands access to primary and secondary preventive services via expansion of telehealth service availability, implementation of 
mobile/satellite service. Focuses on removing geographic barriers to ensure individuals receive necessary medical attention. Replicates 
proven outreach strategies to connect underserved populations with essential clinical providers.
Making Rural Healthcare 
Resilient
Builds and strengthens collaborations among rural health facilities and other local providers to improve operational efficiency. Promotes 
the active adoption of shared service models, co-located community hubs, and integrated networks. Establishes robust regional 
partnerships to help these smaller facilities survive and thrive.

Arkansas
State Snapshot: 2026 Release

Arkansas $207.79M
Budget Period 2 
Funding
Initiative Name Initiative Description
Healthy Eating, Active 
Recreation, & 
Transformation (HEART)
Improves the health of rural Arkansans by increasing access to nutritious foods, physical activities, and preventative healthcare services. 
This effort leverages successful national strategies like the Centers for Disease Control’s (CDC) "Active People, Healthy Nation" program 
alongside existing local community health coalition frameworks. Success relies on critical partnerships with local public-school systems, 
community-based faith organizations, regional food distribution networks, and local healthcare clinics.
Promoting Access, 
Coordination, and 
Transformation (PACT)
Strengthens healthcare delivery in rural Arkansas by expanding specialty care, preventive screenings, and telehealth services. This 
program builds upon locally driven Clinically Integrated Networks (CIN) to encourage collaboration and resource sharing amongrural 
hospitals and clinics. Success relies on deep partnerships with state agencies, rural hospitals, local clinics, community organizations, 
and professional licensing boards 
Recruitment, Innovation, 
Skills, and Education for 
Arkansas (RISE AR)
Builds a strong and skilled healthcare workforce to serve the unique needs of rural Arkansas families. This initiative expands medical 
residency programs and professional training slots by mimicking successful graduate medical education frameworks. Execution relies on 
critical partnerships with local medical schools, rural hospital systems, community health clinics, and state licensing boards.
T elehealth, Health-
Monitoring, and Response 
Innovation for Vital 
Expansion (THRIVE)
Expands digital healthcare access across rural communities through advanced remote monitoring systems and emergency dispatch 
upgrades. This strategy modernizes existing emergency medical infrastructure and utilizes home health technology to support patients 
managing chronic diseases. Success relies on crucial partnerships with local paramedics, rural healthcare networks, emergency
response coordinators, and technology providers.

B.2. Health and 
Lifestyle 
Rural populations are disproportionately impacted by chronic diseases. 
Promoting prevention through physical activity and proper nutrition can 
reduce overall cost of care burden and improve health outcomes. This State 
Policy Action captures whether a State requires schools to reestablish 
the Presidential Fitness Test.
At baseline, AR did not 
require schools to 
reestablish the 
Presidential Fitness Test
AR committed to 
this State Policy 
Action
Complete $2.28M
B.3. SNAP 
Waivers
The Supplemental Nutrition Assistance Program (SNAP) provides food 
benefits to low-income families to supplement their grocery budget. 
Restricting the use of SNAP benefits on non -nutritious foods can help 
improve dietary intake and clinical indicators associated with long -term 
disease in rural populations. This State Policy Action captures State 
efforts to obtain a USDA SNAP Food Restriction Waiver to prohibit 
purchase of non-nutritious items.
At baseline, AR had a 
waiver approved by USDA
No State 
Commitment 
(Waiver in place)
N/A $6.64M
B.4. Nutrition 
Continuing 
Medical 
Education
Improved nutrition education among physicians through continuing medical 
education (CME) requirements can directly contribute to improving the 
health of Americans who live in rural areas given the disproportionate 
impact of chronic disease on rural America. This State Policy Action 
captures State efforts to require nutrition to be a component for CME.
At baseline, AR did not 
require nutrition to be 
included in CME for 
physicians
AR committed to 
this State Policy 
Action
No Progress $2.69M

C.3. Certificate 
of Need
Certificate of Need (CON) laws require healthcare providers to seek 
permission from the State before they can open new facilities or expand 
service offerings. Eliminating or loosening CON laws allows providers to 
establish new facilities in underserved rural regions without increased 
burden, addressing the scarcity of local care options. This State Policy 
Action captures State efforts to ease CON laws across several service 
and facility types.
At baseline, AR had limited 
CON regulations
No State 
Commitment N/A $2.16M
D.2. Licensure 
Compacts
Licensure compacts provide clinicians with the opportunity to serve 
patients across State borders, increasing the supply of accessible rural 
health providers. This State Policy Action captures State participation 
in licensure compacts for physicians, nursing, EMS, psychology, and 
Physician Assistants.
At baseline, AR was a 
member of four of the five 
licensure compacts
AR committed to 
this State Policy 
Action
In Progress:
Physician 
Compact
$2.23M
D.3. Scope of 
Practice
Physician supply challenges in rural areas could be mitigated by 
expanding the scope of practice of other clinicians who have training and 
competency in caring for many of the cases currently limited to physician 
care. This State Policy Action captures State efforts to expand scope 
of practice for Physician Assistants, Nurse Practitioners, 
Pharmacists, and Dental Hygienists.

(SOP) for PAs, reduced SOP 
for NPS, limited authority for 
Pharmacists, and semi -
restricted SOP for Dental 
Hygienists

E.3. Short-term, 
limited-duration 
insurance 
(STLDI)
Rural populations consistently have higher uninsurance rates than their 
urban counterparts. Short -term, limited duration insurance (STLDI) plans 
may offer some rural individuals lower -cost, short-term coverage options. 
This State Policy Action captures whether a State restricts availability, 
use, or timeframe of STLDI beyond the latest federal guidance.

restrict STLDI plans beyond 
the latest federal guidance

California

California $231.11M
Budget Period 2 
Funding
Initiative Name Initiative Description
Transformative Care Model 
Initiative
Improves healthcare access for families and elderly populations in rural California by establishing regional hub networks andintegrated 
telehealth programs. Leverages two established models of behavioral health integration within primary and maternity settings to 
streamline care coordination. Collaborates with essential community partners, including midwives, doulas, and community health 
workers, to expand and upskill the local medical workforce.
Workforce Development 
Initiative
Develops a sustainable, locally rooted healthcare workforce with a primary focus on Tribal, rural, and frontier communities. Strengthens 
active partnerships with community colleges and universities to prepare students for medical careers through structured pathways and 
mentorship. Mandates a five-year service commitment for participants to ensure long-term retention of skilled professionals in 
underserved areas.
T echnology & Tools 
Initiative
Equips rural healthcare providers with modernized technical systems and infrastructure to support primary, maternity, and specialty care. 
Facilitates collaborative purchasing agreements and shared technology services among regional networks to optimize operational 
efficiency. Provides targeted grant funding and specialized technical assistance to overcome local disparities in telehealth utilization and 
data access.

Colorado
State Snapshot: 2026 Release

Colorado $197.16M
Budget Period 2 
Funding
Initiative Name Initiative Description
Transforming Rural Care: 
Hospitals and Chronic 
Disease Prevention
Focuses on providing educational, disease prevention, and care coordination services for high-priority chronic conditions. This initiative 
supports rural hospitals and providers by helping them hire and train staff dedicated to improving care coordination. The program leverages 
partnerships with rural and critical access hospitals, rural health clinics and providers, Federally Qualified Health Centers(FQHCs), 
community-based organizations, Colorado Social Health Information Exchange (CoSHIE) hubs, regional health alliances, workforce 
development partners, and organizations offering training and technical assistance to deliver high-quality prevention services.
Build Data and Evaluation 
Infrastructure for Chronic 
Disease Programs
Strengthens data systems and performance monitoring to ensure chronic disease programs are effective and sustainable. This initiative 
creates a unified approach for collecting and analyzing data across programs and regions, enabling real-time feedback. It supports rural 
providers by providing technical assistance to ensure data interoperability and alignment with state and federal standards and implements 
a standardized performance monitoring and evaluation framework to track reach, outcomes, and return on investment for rural prevention 
programs.
Build and Connect 
Colorado’s Rural Health 
Networks
Establishes coordination and collaboration across Colorado's rural health system by building regional health networks. This initiative 
connects hospitals, clinics, and local public health agencies to improve access, quality, and long-term system resilience. It supports Tribal 
participation by helping them create their own rural health networks.
Strengthen Rural Care 
Delivery Systems
Transforms rural-care delivery systems by improving EMS coverage and expanding care coordination. This initiative enhances the capacity 
of rural providers to deliver timely and sustainable high-quality care in their communities. It supports collaborative care networks that 
integrate rural hospitals, rural health clinics, and behavioral health providers with shared electronic medical record systems, referral 
management, care coordination positions, and joint protocols for acute-care transitions.
Sustain Rural Hospital 
Operations and Regulatory 
Readiness
Ensures the long-term stability and operational capacity of rural hospitals across Colorado. This initiative provides direct financial support 
to rural hospitals facing financial, operational, and workforce challenges. It enables hospitals to procure technical assistance and targeted 
operational support to realign or sustain essential service lines, enable regulatory readiness, invest in small-scale infrastructure 
improvements, and support strategic planning and implementation. 
$200.11M
Budget Period 1 
Funding

Colorado
Initiative Name Initiative Description
Strengthen and Expand the 
Rural Health Workforce
Strengthens Colorado’s rural health workforce by expanding access to training and continuing education opportunities for health 
professionals and other health workers serving in identified Health Professional Shortage Areas (HPSAs). Funding will be usedto attract, 
train, and retain local essential providers (e.g., physicians, nurses, pharmacists, qualified behavioral health assistants, health workers, 
technicians, and others) who are critical to improving chronic disease prevention and management and sustaining rural care delivery. 
Incentive payments are performance-based and support activities like workforce recruitment, training, and retention.
Expanding Clinical Capacity 
to Perform Preventive Care
Increases the clinical workforce's capacity to deliver preventive services and procedures in rural and frontier communities. This initiative 
equips rural clinicians with relevant chronic disease-focused training to help them meet preventive screening needs in their regions. It is 
administered through partnerships with rural hospitals, medical providers, and academic institutions.
Strengthening State and 
Local Health Coordination
Enhances coordination and alignment between state, regional, and local health entities to ensure effective implementation of prevention 
and workforce programs. This initiative invests in technical assistance and training to strengthen collaborative governance and ensure a 
sustained rural health workforce. It supports "Grow Your Own" (GYO) programs by establishing inter-state and regional collaboration 
practices.
Design and Pilot Rural 
Value-Based Care Model(s)
Researches, designs, and pilots value-based care models tailored for rural providers and hospitals. This initiative assesses thefeasibility of 
shared savings, bundled payments, and other approaches that reward prevention and care coordination. The program is administered
through a collaboration between the Colorado Department of Health Care Policy and Financing (HCPF), rural health clinics, andRegional 
Accountable Entities (RAEs).
Expand Rural T elehealth and 
T echnology Integration
Strengthens rural health system participation in the use of technology-enabled chronic disease prevention, monitoring, and care delivery. 
This initiative equips rural providers with the tools and technical support needed to engage in data-driven, integrated care. Itsupports 
projects that conduct statewide technology readiness assessment, local health organization to expand mobile health programs, and
integrated dashboards that maintain data tools to access rural population data and program outcomes.

Connecticut
State Snapshot: 2026 Release

Connecticut $153.65M
Budget Period 2 
Funding
Initiative Name Initiative Description
Population Health 
Outcomes
Facilitates access to prevention, primary care, dental, and behavioral health. Builds simple entry points to care in community settings by 
strengthening the capacity of primary care clinics, community health centers, rural health clinics, behavioral health providers, tele-
behavioral services, EMS and community teams, schools, senior centers, parks, and other local organizations. Supports mobile health 
delivery, operationalizing new primary care and dental vans for rural and Tribal communities. Establishes a local facilitatoror hub to 
convene partners and implement strategies that promote access to school-based mental health resources and referral, expand preventive 
services for older adults, promote active living through community assets, and strengthen behavioral health support. Improvesaccess to 
mental health supports and emergency department (ED) diversion for adults. Extends the Family Bridge universal nurse and community 
health worker home visiting program for newborns and postpartum women. 
Workforce
Expands education and training opportunities, aligning licensure and certification standards, and improving recruitment and retention 
across levels of care. Builds accessible training programs with certifications that transfer easily between care settings. Expands clinical 
placements, modernizing training curricula, and removing barriers to employment in rural areas. Invests in coordinated strategies that 
strengthen training pipelines, remove practice barriers to education, and enhance career development opportunities. 
Data and Technology 
Expands statewide Health Information Exchange (HIE) participation, building shared technology infrastructure, and supporting innovative 
data tools that connect providers, improve care coordination, and ensure access to modern health systems for rural residents.Through 
collaboration with the Office of Policy and Management (formerly Office of Health Strategy (OHS)), Department of Public Health (DPH), 
Department of Social Services (DSS), and Connecticut’s statewide HIE, Connie, the initiative creates a fully integrated data ecosystem that 
enables providers to share information, track outcomes, and respond quickly to patient needs. Hospitals, Federally Qualified Health 
Centers (FQHCs), long-term care providers, behavioral health providers, and EMS will be better connected through shared tools for 
population health management, event notification, and predictive analytics. These systems will improve care coordination, reduce
unnecessary ED use, and support proactive outreach and chronic disease management in rural settings.
Care Transformation and 
Stability 
Strengthens the foundation of rural healthcare by supporting infrastructure and right-sizing support, improving care coordination, as well as 
supporting value-based models and sustainable financing strategies. Supports integrated systems of care that expand access to 
community-based services, reduce reliance on hospital-based interventions, and improve financial resilience for local providers.Supports 
the development of 23-hour adult crisis stabilization units that provide short-term, non-hospital crisis care in safe, home-like environments.

Delaware
State Snapshot: 2026 Release

Delaware $160.29M
Budget Period 2 
Funding
Initiative Name Initiative Description
Rural “Hope Center” 
Initiative
Replicates the successful Hope Center model to serve rural residents experiencing homelessness with serious mental illness and 
substance use disorder in Kent and Sussex Counties. Establishes two new centers in through the strategic renovation, staffing, and 
equipping of existing state-owned facilities, transforming underutilized infrastructure into comprehensive, integrated hubs for healthcare, 
behavioral health, and housing stability services. These centers will offer critical non congregate temporary housing, including at least 20 
transitional beds specifically for individuals with serious mental illness and substance use disorder, plus safe individual and family units. 
The centers will strengthen the rural service network and support upstream drivers of health by bringing essential health, behavioral health, 
and housing supports together in communities where access to these services are limited.
Rural Community Health 
Hubs 
Establishes a network of mobile health units and brick-and-mortar community health hubs to deliver preventive, chronic, and specialty care 
to underserved communities in rural Sussex and Kent Counties. Awards fund local health systems, community organizations, and health 
technology partners to deploy staff, equip, and operate mobile health units and brick-and-mortar community health hubs. Mobile health 
units will be strategically deployed to familiar, accessible community locations including schools, churches, town centers, and community 
parking lots, bringing medical services directly to rural residents where they live and gather, especially for physically disabled and elderly 
residents.
School-Based Health 
Centers Expansion Initiative 
Expands Delaware’s network of school-based health centers (SBHCs) by establishing four new SBHCs in rural Sussex County middle 
schools to provide comprehensive physical, behavioral, and preventive health services directly to students, eliminating transportation 
barriers and improving access to primary care, mental health services, behavioral risk assessments, chronic disease management, and 
nutrition counseling. Supports minor building renovations to establish clinical space (exam rooms, counseling offices, screening areas), 
purchase equipment and supplies, and establish operations.
Food is Medicine (FIM) 
Infrastructure Initiative 
Institutes a FIM infrastructure in rural Kent and Sussex counties to address high rates of hypertension and diabetes among food-insecure 
older adults. Hires 4-5 community health workers and registered dietitians to serve patients in rural health practices across Kent and 
Sussex. Deploys Electronic Medical Record (EMR)-decision support tools for participating providers, a telehealth nutrition consultation 
platform, a patient-facing mobile application, and remote patient monitoring systems. Certifies 15 Culinary Medicine Teachers totrain 150 
rural practitioners (70% rural serving) and implements of a pilot for 375 participants with diabetes or hypertension. Develops coding, billing, 
and payment approaches to integrate FIM services into Delaware Medicaid and value-based care arrangements. Pilots a high school work-
based learning program for students to gain hands-on experience in FIM interventions.
$157.39M
Budget Period 1 
Funding

Delaware
Initiative Name Initiative Description
Rural Libraries Health 
Access Initiative 
Expands health access services and operating hours at nine rural libraries in Kent and Sussex Counties. Leverages the existing library 
infrastructure to provide telehealth, health navigation, and social service support to residents. Newly hired library-based community health 
specialists will assist rural residents with scheduling and completing virtual health visits, supporting health insurance applications 
(Medicare, Medicaid), facilitating Electronic Benefits Transfer (EBT)/ Supplemental Nutrition Assistance Program (SNAP)benefits 
applications, providing referrals to community partners, offering courses on health literacy, and delivering other health programming. Also 
connects residents to workforce readiness coaching and legal assistance services.
Rural Provider and Federally 
Qualified Health Centers 
(FQHC) Value-Based Care 
Readiness Initiative 
Accelerates the transition of rural providers and FQHCs to value-based care models through three tracks: (1) direct awards to Delaware’s 
three FQHCs for innovative technology investments that reduce administrative burden and promote patient centered data exchange, and 
for high-value services and care teams; (2) competitive awards to non-FQHC rural providers and community organizations; and (3) 
statewide infrastructure and a sustainable framework for collaboration between providers and payers. 
Catalyst Fund for T elehealth 
and Remote Monitoring 
Encourages the development and adoption of health technology solutions for rural populations. The fund will competitively select health 
technology companies to develop and deploy novel solutions to prevent disease, manage chronic conditions, and improve health 
outcomes. Products may include remote patient monitoring, wearable devices, proven AI-enabled sensors for wound and infection risk 
detection, conversational AI for wellness monitoring and social connection, and hospital-at-home monitoring systems.
Rural Delaware Diabetes 
Wellness Pilot Program 
Launches a three-year pilot study to improve diabetes care for 500 patients in rural Delaware. Assesses the impact of continuousglucose 
monitoring (CGM) and hands-on care management. This program monitors whether real-time adjustments in health behaviors (e.g., diet, 
exercise, eating whole foods) paired with appropriate medication use improves health outcomes. Also expands diabetic retinopathy
screening and provides evidence-based prevention services, health education, and coordinated support to individuals at risk of developing 
Type 2 diabetes.
Delaware Medical School 
Establishes a "Primary Care – Rural Health" track medical school program in Delaware focused on the health priorities most critical to rural 
populations: community medicine, preventive care through lifestyle changes, substance use disorder treatment, behavioral health, and 
integration of care with community providers. The initiative will leverage Delaware's existing healthcare infrastructure for clinical training 
and is  the foundation of Delaware's comprehensive 'Train Here, Stay Here' workforce development strategy.
$160.29M
Budget Period 2 
Funding
$157.39M
Budget Period 1 
Funding

Delaware
Initiative Name Initiative Description
Medical School Rural 
Workforce Development 
Program 
Provides comprehensive financial awards to medical students who commit to five years of practice and caring for Delaware’s rural
population as part of the 'Train Here, Stay Here' workforce development strategy. The initiative will support two pathways: 1) students 
enrolled in Delaware’s new medical school’s Primary Care – Rural Health track, and 2) students who complete their third- and fourth-year 
clinical rotations in Delaware, gaining exposure to the state’s communities and rural healthcare needs.
Rural Medical Residency 
Recruitment Program 
Provides awards for wraparound services, short-term housing, and relocation expenses to medical residents training in rural Delaware who 
commit to five years of service to rural Delawareans. Serves as the third component of Delaware's 'Train Here, Stay Here' workforce 
development strategy. Provides seamless transition support, maintaining financial assistance as they move from medical schoolto
residency training—all while deepening their commitment to Delaware's rural communities.
Training Programs for 
Clinical Support Roles in 
Rural Areas 
Expands clinical training for Non-Physician Practitioners, “physician extenders” , community health workers, dental professionals,  and 
clinical support staff in rural Delaware. Funds health systems, educational institutions, community organizations, and other partners to 
create new programs, apprenticeships, and fellowships for a broad array of healthcare professionals including physician assistants, 
nurses, midwives, paramedics, phlebotomists, laboratory technicians, occupational therapists, nutritionists, long-term care staff, dental 
hygienists, dental assistants, dentists-in-training, and community health workers (CHWs) providing health education, preventive care, and 
care coordination. 
Rural Health Workforce 
Education Program
Provides direct financial awards to trainees enrolled in clinical and non-clinical training programs who commit to five years ofservice in 
Delaware's underserved rural communities. Works in tandem with other initiatives to build the healthcare workforce by reducing financial 
barriers for trainees. Awards are distributed through competitively selected clinical training institutions that also receivefunding under the 
Training Programs for Clinical Support Roles initiative.
$160.29M
Budget Period 2 
Funding
$157.39M
Budget Period 1 
Funding

Delaware
Initiative Name Initiative Description
Healthcare Workforce Data 
Center Initiative 
Establishes a dedicated data center to collect and analyze real-time data on Delaware's healthcare workforce. Monitors employment status 
and activity, practice settings and payment types, Medicaid and Medicare acceptance, time spent seeing patients, and other critical 
indicators that reveal the true state of healthcare across Delaware. Insights derived from this database will inform dashboards and annual 
reports that focus on identifying where workforce shortages remain, which specialties are underrepresented, and how demographic or 
geographic disparities impact access to care.
Statewide Health 
Information Technology 
Infrastructure for Real-Time 
Insurance Verification and 
Prior Authorizations 
Creates comprehensive digital infrastructure connecting all rural providers, payers, hospital systems, and patients through Delaware 
Health Information Network (DHIN). Accelerates prior authorization processing by allowing providers to exchange data electronically and 
allowing payers to report faster authorization decisions. Cloud-based infrastructure minimizes maintenance and operations management, 
while vendor-neutral standards prevent vendor lock-in and stakeholder councils ensure fair governance. Directly improves rural health by 
eliminating geographic barriers to specialist consultations, reducing the need for multiple trips, and supporting telehealth and remote 
patient monitoring that depend on seamless data exchange.
Delaware is also implementing a Rural T echnology Catalyst Fund, which promotes consumer-facing, technology-driven solutions for chronic disease prevention 
and management in rural populations.

C.3. Certificate 
of Need
Certificate of Need (CON) laws require healthcare providers to seek 
permission from the State before they can open new facilities or expand 
service offerings. Eliminating or loosening CON laws allows providers to 
establish new facilities in underserved rural regions without increased 
burden, addressing the scarcity of local care options. This State Policy 
Action captures State efforts to ease CON laws across several service 
and facility types.
At baseline, DE had 
moderate CON 
regulations

Florida

Florida $203.83M
Budget Period 2 
Funding
Initiative Name Initiative Description
Rural and Satellite Clinics
Provides start-up funding for established urban physician or dental practices to set up new or satellite offices in rural areas.Supports 
clinics run by nurse practitioners and physician assistants to offer primary, dental, and mental healthcare to local residents. Uses secure 
electronic health records and tracking networks to improve patient follow-up and coordinate overall care.
Mobile Health
Delivers preventive care, health screenings, and prenatal services directly to rural communities using mobile medical units on scheduled 
routes. Partners with local hospitals, clinics, and community groups to make sure patients do not experience gaps in their care. Connects 
mobile units to secure electronic databases so doctors can quickly update patient charts and track health progress.
Community Paramedicine
Deploys trained community paramedics and licensed practitioners to treat minor illnesses and provide home care to patients who were 
recently discharged from the hospital, reducing preventable readmissions. Uses telehealth services and digital notification systems to 
coordinate care between emergency workers and primary care teams. Helps patients manage their medications, provides health 
education, and links individuals to behavioral health specialists.
Behavioral Health 
T elehealth & T elehub
Psychiatry
Establishes regional psychiatric centers to connect rural residents with same-day mental health therapy and consultations. Supports 
local primary care doctors by using a collaborative care model where specialists help create shared treatment and medicine plans. Trains 
staff on standardized screening tools and digital technology to track patient progress and make data-driven decisions.
T ele-Specialties & Imaging 
(Virtual Specialty Clinics)
Enables rural hospitals and clinics to host virtual appointments where patients can consult with specialists including cardiologists or 
oncologists via video. Relies on local nurses to perform physical exams and securely send test results to the remote specialists. 
Integrates cloud-based imaging systems to speed up medical diagnoses and eliminate the need for long-distance travel.
$209.94M
Budget Period 1 
Funding

Florida
Initiative Name Initiative Description
T ele–Intensive Care Unit 
(eICU)
Connects rural hospitals with remote critical care doctors and nurses who monitor patients around the clock through secure video and 
data feeds. Supports virtual nursing and medicine management to keep critically ill patients safe and close to their families. Integrates 
directly with hospital records to speed up response times and prevent unnecessary transfers to distant facilities.
Hub-and-Spoke T elestroke
Connects rural emergency rooms with remote stroke specialists using real-time video for immediate patient evaluations. Transmitsbrain 
scans electronically to neurologists so they can quickly diagnose the patient and recommend treatment. Follows standardized clinical 
guidelines to help local staff give life-saving medications safely and decide if a transfer is necessary.
Workforce Development
Strengthens the rural medical pipeline by funding clinical training programs, housing assistance, and recruitment efforts fornew 
practitioners. Builds career pathways in local high schools and community colleges to interest students in health fields. Requires program 
participants to commit to a five-year service period in a rural area to ensure long-term staffing stability.
Health and Lifestyle
Addresses food insecurity and nutrition issues by adding food screenings and basic counseling services into rural clinics. Educates 
healthcare providers on how to give nutrition advice and works with schools to promote physical activity. Teaches middle and high school 
students about healthy eating behaviors to reduce chronic diseases across the population.
Remote Patient 
T elemonitoring
Allows rural patients with chronic conditions like diabetes or high blood pressure to use connected devices to transmit theirdaily health 
data to doctors. Uses an artificial intelligence system to look for warning signs in the data and alert nurses when a patientneeds help. 
Prevents unnecessary hospitalizations and emergency room visits by catching health issues early.
$203.83M
Budget Period 2 
Funding
$209.94M
Budget Period 1 
Funding

Florida
Initiative Name Initiative Description
Value-Based Purchasing 
(VBP)
Transitions rural healthcare providers to payment models that reward the quality and efficiency of care rather than the volume of services. 
Helps clinics earn medical home designations to improve chronic disease management and coordinate patient care. Secures the 
financial future of rural clinics by offering incentive payments when providers meet health quality benchmarks.
Retail Clinic Services
Expands healthcare access by reimbursing rural pharmacies that offer basic medical care and routine health screenings within their 
scope of practice. Features diagnostic telehealth kiosks inside pharmacies to connect patients with remote doctors for same-day 
treatment. Utilizes existing business locations to fill healthcare gaps and reduce unnecessary visits to local emergency rooms.
Florida Health Information 
Exchange / Encounter 
Notification Service 
(HIE/ENS) Onboarding
Funds the connection of rural hospitals and clinics to statewide health information and notification systems. Sends real-time alerts to 
doctors when a patient is admitted, transferred, or discharged, which helps lower hospital readmission rates and enhance care
coordination. Modernizes communication technology by training staff and updating software platforms to meet state standards.
Diagnostics Technology 
Support
Equips rural clinics and labs with cloud-based imaging systems to allow rapid sharing of x-rays and laboratory tests. Improves 
communication between rural facilities and distant specialists to speed up patient diagnoses. Supports staff training, digital security 
upgrades, and record system integrations to make clinical decision-making faster and safer.
Integrated Medicare-
Medicaid Plans Education 
and Outreach
Increases awareness and enrollment in integrated health plans among rural Florida residents who qualify for both Medicare andMedicaid. 
Directs targeted education and training to local stakeholders to help people sign up for plans that combine their medical benefits. 
Simplifies access to care and reduces duplicate services for patients with complex medical and social needs.

Waivers

grocery budget. Restricting the use of SNAP benefits on non -nutritious foods 
can help improve dietary intake and clinical indicators associated with long -

efforts to obtain a USDA SNAP Food Restriction Waiver to prohibit 
purchase of non-nutritious items.

waiver approved by USDA

Georgia

Georgia $221.73M
Budget Period 2 
Funding
Initiative Name Initiative Description
Transforming for a 
Sustainable Health System
Prepares rural healthcare facilities and providers to qualify for the national Achieving Healthcare Efficiency through Accountable Design 
(AHEAD) model for value-based care. This initiative focuses on achieving long-term financial stability for rural hospitals by assessing 
readiness, identifying gaps, and providing technical support and grants. It involves collaboration with various providers, hospitals, and 
community organizations to transform how healthcare is delivered and paid for in rural areas.
Strengthening the 
Continuum of Care
Addresses healthcare gaps for rural Georgians across all life stages. This initiative improves the health of children and families by creating 
better links between schools, community behavioral health, and primary care providers. It also expands crucial services like newborn 
screenings, emergency transportation, support for brain injury survivors, and increases access to nutrition services for children with 
autism spectrum disorder (ASD) and pregnant women. 
Connecting to Care to 
Improve Healthcare Access
Closes gaps in access to quality healthcare for rural populations through innovation and technology. This effort deploys mobile health 
clinics and telehealth pods to bring primary, specialty, dental, behavioral, and preventive care directly to communities. It also strengthens 
perinatal care and enhances telehealth services through partnerships with rural hospitals, clinics, and public health departments.
Growing a Highly Skilled 
Healthcare Workforce
Strengthens the healthcare workforce in rural Georgia through focused education, training, and retention programs. The initiative provides 
educational awards, residencies, and financial incentives to encourage doctors, nurses, and other clinicians to practice in rural areas. It 
uses partnerships with academic institutions and health systems to build a sustainable pipeline of skilled providers for underserved 
communities.
Leveraging T echnology for 
Healthcare Innovations
Creates a modern and efficient healthcare system by integrating new technologies. This initiative improves patient data security, 
enhances electronic medical records, and provides funding for new health technologies incorporating solutions such as Artificial
Intelligence (AI), cybersecurity, robotics, and electronic medical records. It supports rural hospitals and clinics by using technology to 
improve patient care, expand access, and increase data sharing among providers.
Rural Technology Catalyst Fund to be implemented in Y ear 2

Remote care services can help expand access to care by allowing 
clinicians to provide care to rural residents from another location via 
telehealth, remote patient monitoring, or other modalities. This State 
Policy Action captures State Medicaid coverage of remote care 
services and the presence of other policies to allow out -of-State 
clinicians to provide telehealth/remote care.
At baseline, GA Medicaid 
covered two types of remote 
care services and had 
policies in place to allow 
out-of-State clinicians to 
provide telehealth/remote 
care
GA committed to 
this State Policy 
Action

for Remote 
Patient Monitoring

Hawai’i
State Snapshot: 2026 Release

Hawai’i
Initiative Name Initiative Description
Rural Health Information 
Network (RHIN)
Connects rural hospitals, clinics, and Native Hawaiian health centers to the rest of the state through a modern, statewide digital 
backbone. Integrates interoperable electronic health records, wireless networks, and data hubs to facilitate secure data exchange. 
Enables seamless care coordination and practice transformation across independent providers.
Pili Ola T elehealth Network
Connects rural communities directly to healthcare providers by establishing a comprehensive, statewide telehealth network. Integrates 
digital health access, virtual care, and clinical telehealth training into underserved areas. Improves healthcare delivery bybridging 
geographic barriers to match local patients with specialized medical professionals.
Rural Infrastructure for 
Care Access (RICA)
Expands physical EMS and bolsters the existing behavioral health infrastructure in rural communities. Implements evidence-based 
community care practices by expanding healthcare teams, community paramedicine, and mobile healthcare units. Increases immediate
care access to improve patient outcomes and reduce regional disparities.
Hawai'i Outreach for 
Medical Education in Rural 
Under-resourced 
Neighborhoods (HOME 
RUN)
Expands the education, recruitment, and retention of healthcare workers across rural Hawai'i through a robust pipeline-to-practice 
initiative. Offers tailored certificate programs, residency opportunities, educational awards, and financial incentives to build the local 
workforce. Delivers structured mentoring and professional support to encourage clinicians to establish practices in underserved areas.
$189.62M
Budget Period 2 
Funding
$188.89M
Budget Period 1 
Funding

Hawai’i
Initiative Name Initiative Description
Rural Respite Network 
(RRN)
Expands the effective medical respite model to five rural areas providing safe recovery spaces for unhoused or post-acute patients with 
low medical acuity. The centers will offer medical and behavioral health staffing, primary care, case management, and other 
personal/hygiene services. Reduces preventable hospital use and emergency department overcrowding through targeted, community-
based care.
Rural Value-Based 
Innovation (RVBI) & AHEAD 
Readiness Fund
Finances local value-based innovations to prepare rural providers to succeed under the Achieving Healthcare Efficiency through 
Accountable Design (AHEAD) model. Establishes a competitive grant fund to enable clinics and hospitals to adopt innovative care delivery 
models. Aligns financial incentives with patient outcomes to secure long-term viability for rural healthcare facilities.

B.3. SNAP 
Waivers
The Supplemental Nutrition Assistance Program (SNAP) provides food 
benefits to low-income families to supplement their grocery budget. 
Restricting the use of SNAP benefits on non -nutritious foods can help 
improve dietary intake and clinical indicators associated with long -term 
disease in rural populations. This State Policy Action captures State 
efforts to obtain a USDA SNAP Food Restriction Waiver to prohibit 
purchase of non-nutritious items.
At baseline, HI had 
submitted a waiver, being 
processed by USDA
HI committed to 
this State Policy 
Action
Complete $6.64M
C.3. Certificate 
of Need
Certificate of Need (CON) laws require healthcare providers to seek 
permission from the State before they can open new facilities or expand 
service offerings. Eliminating or loosening CON laws allows providers to 
establish new facilities in underserved rural regions without increased 
burden, addressing the scarcity of local care options. This State Policy 
Action captures State efforts to ease CON laws across several service 
and facility types.
At baseline, HI had 
moderate CON regulations
No State 
Commitment N/A $1.44M

Idaho $182.70M
Budget Period 2 
Funding
Initiative Name Initiative Description
Improving Rural Access T o 
Care Through Technology
Strengthens the foundation of rural care through investments in telehealth, shared infrastructure, and cybersecurity. This initiative improves 
care delivery for rural citizens by equipping healthcare facilities like Rural Health Clinics (RHCs), Critical Access Hospitals (CAHs), and 
Federally Qualified Health Centers (FQHCs) with modern technology. It requires collaboration between healthcare systems, treatment 
centers, and long-term care facilities to successfully implement these upgrades.
Ensuring Accessible Quality 
Care Through Innovative 
Models
Extends healthcare services to geographically isolated areas using innovative and technology enabled care models. This effortuses health 
extenders, such as community health workers and community health emergency medical services (CHEMS) professionals, to providecare 
in home and community settings. The initiative relies on partnerships with EMS agencies, post secondary institutions, and community 
employers to expand the healthcare workforce.
Sustaining Rural Workforce 
With Training, Recruitment, 
And Retention
Builds a skilled rural health workforce by offering financial incentives, new training pathways, and community support. The initiative targets 
a wide range of healthcare professionals, from students to established providers, by offering incentives including educational awards, 
retention bonuses, and expanded medical education programs. Success depends on partnerships with educational institutions, healthcare 
facilities, and community-based organizations.
Implement Population 
Specific, Evidence-Based 
Projects To Make America 
Healthy Again
Advances rural health by implementing prevention and treatment programs for chronic disease, behavioral health, and maternal and child 
health. This initiative serves rural populations by using proven, evidence-based models to improve health outcomes and build local 
capacity for care. It requires partnerships with a broad range of stakeholders, including healthcare providers, school systems, and 
community organizations.
Investing In Rural Health 
Infrastructure And 
Partnerships
Addresses the need for sustainable access to rural healthcare through investments in facility renovations, clinical equipment, and 
partnerships. The initiative focuses on modernizing healthcare facilities to support innovative care delivery and specializedservices for 
rural communities. Key partnerships include tribal nations, pharmacies, and various healthcare systems and clinics.

limited-duration 
insurance 
(STLDI)
Rural populations consistently have higher uninsurance rates than their 
urban counterparts. Short -term, limited duration insurance (STLDI) plans 
may offer some rural individuals lower -cost, short-term coverage options. 
This State Policy Action captures whether a State restricts availability, 
use, or timeframe of STLDI beyond the latest federal guidance.

restrict STLDI plans 
beyond the latest federal 
guidance

Illinois
State Snapshot: 2026 Release

Illinois
Initiative Name Initiative Description
Hospital Transformation
Modernizes the rural healthcare delivery system to improve the long-term financial viability of rural hospitals. This initiativebuilds upon 
the Illinois' Health Transformation Collaboratives (HTCs) model to establish regional partnerships, transform service lines, and develop 
sustainable, high quality care models for rural residents. Key stakeholders include the four existing rural HTCs, rural hospitals, and various 
healthcare providers and community organizations.
Community Care 
Infrastructure
Strengthens primary and behavioral healthcare in rural regions to reduce fragmentation and improve access to care. This initiative 
provides targeted funding to providers to implement integrated, team-based care models, such as the Collaborative Care Model (CoCM), 
and enhance care coordination. It requires partnerships with organizations like the Illinois Primary Health Care Association (IPHCA) and 
the Community Behavioral Health Association (CBHA) to distribute funds and provide oversight
Hospital Disease 
Prevention
Supports rural hospitals in implementing disease prevention programming for the communities they serve. This initiative directs hospitals 
to use community health needs assessments to identify local health priorities and deploy evidence-based interventions for conditions 
like diabetes and mental health. The Illinois Critical Access Hospital Network (ICAHN) will administer funds to rural hospitals based on 
the population size of their service area.
EMS and Mobile Healthcare
Addresses the lack of available transportation and healthcare shortages in rural areas by funding mobile healthcare models. This initiative 
enhances rural EMS by expanding services like community paramedicine, leveraging the Chicago EMS Mobile Integrated Healthcare
Community Paramedicine (MIH-CP) model. It involves partnerships with the Illinois Department of Public Health (IDPH) and Resource 
Hospitals to build the necessary infrastructure and support for mobile health services.
$190.46
Budget Period 2 
Funding
$193.42M
Budget Period 1 
Funding

Illinois
Initiative Name Initiative Description
T echnological Innovation 
for Virtual Care
Ensures that individuals in rural areas have consistent access to specialty care through technology. This initiative supportstechnological 
innovation, led by the ICAHN, to strengthen the rural care ecosystem by implementing solutions like telehealth, cybersecurity, and 
electronic health records (EHR) modernization. The program will assess the digital readiness of rural hospitals and provide funding to 
adopt new, vetted technologies.
Healthcare Workforce 
Expansion
Strengthens healthcare delivery in rural communities by attracting and retaining health professionals in high need specialties. This 
initiative provides funding to state universities, community colleges, and hospitals for educational award programs, bonuses for
relocation in rural areas, and access to other training programs. The program will focus on providers who commit to serving in rural 
communities for at least five years.
Training Healthcare Support 
Workers
Addresses gaps in non-physician healthcare roles by expanding training and certification programs tailored to local needs. This initiative 
focuses on developing a larger workforce for roles such as Community Health Workers (CHWs), doulas, and peer support professionals to 
enhance care in rural communities. Funding will be distributed through the Illinois Community College Board (ICCB) and the University of 
Illinois to establish and strengthen partnerships between community colleges and local health systems.
Rural Health Education 
Pipeline
Expands career pathways for rural high school students to build a sustainable local healthcare workforce. This initiative leverages the 
University of Illinois Chicago College of Medicine (UIC) Area Health Education Center (AHEC) program to provide students withhands on 
experiences, job shadowing, and mentorship encompassing clinical and non-clinical careers. It will foster partnerships between AHEC, 
rural hospitals, and schools to create a pipeline of future healthcare professionals in rural areas.

Indiana
State Snapshot: 2026 Release

Indiana $208.91M
Budget Period 2 
Funding
Initiative Name Initiative Description
Growing Care Coordination: 
Medical Operations 
Coordination Center 
(MOCC) and Alternate 
Payment Model Feasibility 
Study
Establishes a 24/7 statewide hub to coordinate patient transfers, EMS resources and hospital capacity. The MOCC aims to ensure rural 
communities get timely access to trauma, stroke, psychiatric and maternal care by streamlining referrals, reducing inappropriate
emergency room (ER) use, supporting rural hospital sustainability and strengthening preparedness for mass casualty events. Italso 
studies new payment models to improve financial stability for rural healthcare providers.
Growing Community 
Connections through 
Indiana 211
Launches Indiana Community Connect, a coordinated care network designed to help people in rural communities access essential 
resources more easily. The initiative brings together healthcare providers, community organizations, and Indiana 211 to connect 
individuals with services such as food assistance, housing support, and behavioral health programs. This work strengthens coordination 
and follow up in areas where local services are limited, with the goal of improving stability and well-being for rural residents.
Growing Improved Patient 
Outcomes through 
Enhanced Interoperability 
and Technology
Modernizes Indiana’s health information exchange (HIE) to connect rural healthcare facilities (hospitals, outpatient, long-term care, 
behavioral health), close data gaps and strengthen care coordination through electronic medical records (EMR) integration, technology 
enhancements, artificial intelligence (AI)-driven dashboards and EMS collaboration.
Growing Pediatric & 
Obstetric Readiness in 
Rural Emergency 
Departments
Strengthening emergency care capacity in rural hospitals and EMS by ensuring readiness for pediatric and obstetric emergencies. 
Partnering with Indiana Emergency Medical Services for Children (IEMSC) and Indiana Department of Homeland Security (IDHS), the 
initiative provides needs assessments, training, equipment and technical assistance.
$206.93M
Budget Period 1 
Funding

Indiana
Initiative Name Initiative Description
Growing Cardiometabolic 
Health Standards of Care in 
Rural Indiana
Launches a statewide collaborative care model to combat obesity, diabetes and cardiovascular disease by establishing Cardiometabolic 
Centers of Excellence in each rural health region. The initiative equips 6,000 rural health professionals with Lifestyle Medicine training, 
embeds nutrition education in medical schools, and certifies up to 2,000 clinicians to lead preventive care. A feasibility study will also 
assess logistics for a rural Food is Medicine program, leveraging Hoosier-grown foods and telehealth-based nutrition therapy.
Growing Access to Hospital 
Post-Discharge Medications
Ensures patients receive prescribed medications before leaving the hospital, eliminating access and transportation barriers. By charging 
outpatient prices and providing bedside counseling, the program improves recovery readiness and reduces readmissions.
Growing Specialty Provider 
Access through Expanded 
T ele-consult Capabilities
Assesses existing provider networks and specialty gaps while building a secure teleconsultation system to expand access to high-need 
specialties such as psychiatry and behavioral health. By addressing barriers like credentialing and payment incentives, and leveraging AI 
to optimize consultation availability, the initiative strengthens rural healthcare infrastructure. Competitive partnerships will support 
rollout across communities, ensuring sustainable, scalable access to specialty care.
Growing T elehealth Access 
and Infrastructure
Expands telehealth services for Indiana's 64 rural counties by conducting a statewide feasibility study to assess rural provider networks, 
infrastructure gaps, and opportunities for remote health monitoring using wearable devices to reduce clinician burnout and improve 
patient outcomes. Findings will guide the rollout of a unified telehealth system, procured through competitive selection, with solutions 
designed for rural connectivity and enhanced by AI-driven decision support.
$208.91M
Budget Period 2 
Funding
$206.93M
Budget Period 1 
Funding

Indiana
Initiative Name Initiative Description
Growing our Rural Health 
Paraprofessional Workforce
Grows and retains the rural healthcare, behavioral and oral health paraprofessional workforce by establishing career pipelines and 
pathways through Rural Healthcare Academies, providing employer technical assistance and support for employee recruitment and
retention, and supports Community Health Worker planning and integration in clinical and community care settings.
Growing Clinical Training 
and Readiness
Advances rural healthcare access and clinical workforce strength through expanded physician training pathways, increasing clinical 
rotation capacity, and targeted investments in maternal health.
Growing our Rural 
Behavioral Health 
Workforce
Grows and retains the rural behavioral health workforce through career pathways, employer support, and expanded training. This initiative 
will support a university-based career pathway for high school students, offering early exposure and training. To support the current 
workforce, this initiative provides employer grants to enhance recruitment and retention and strengthen clinical supervision.This initiative 
also expands Certified Peer Support Professional training and continuing education and provides statewide workshops to equip rural 
paraprofessionals with critical threat assessment and management skills to improve crisis response and community safety.
Make Rural Indiana Healthy 
Again Regional Grants
Brings the federal RHTP program to the state level. Indiana understands that rural communities know their own needs best and are well-
positioned to create effective local solutions. The state has a strong track record of helping communities use data to identify their biggest 
opportunities and leverage local resources and partnerships to address them.

Iowa
State Snapshot: 2026 Release

Iowa $216.01M
Budget Period 2 
Funding
Initiative Name Initiative Description
Hometown Connections
Strengthens care coordination and service delivery in rural areas through strategic partnerships among healthcare providers. The initiative 
targets rural residents by leveraging partnerships with regional hospitals to address persistent gaps in care. This initiative expands an 
existing hub-and-spoke model, which Iowa refers to as Centers of Excellence. Additionally, new Health Hub and Spoke programs and
school-based services programs are developed. 
Combat Cancer: Prevent 
and Treat
Reduces the burden of cancer in rural communities through investments in prevention, screening, and treatment strategies. This initiative 
expands access to care through a Health Hub model, focusing on lung, breast, colon, and skin cancers. The program leverages 
partnerships and rural provider networks to address longstanding gaps in cancer care.
Communities of Care
Addresses challenges in accessing coordinated, person-centered care by piloting co-located service delivery sites. The initiative 
enhances care coordination by embedding community health workers (CHWs) to help with chronic disease prevention. This program
targets rural Iowans by partnering with local public health agencies, Federally Qualified Health Centers (FQHCs), and hospitals.
Health Information 
Exchange
Sustains and expands the Iowa Health Information Exchange (HIE) to transform rural healthcare delivery. This initiative improves care 
coordination by connecting rural providers to patient data across different electronic medical record platforms. The program targets rural 
hospitals and clinics by leveraging partnerships with behavioral health providers, FQHCs, and local health departments.
Emergency Medical Service 
(EMS) Community Care 
Mobile
Transforms prehospital care by strengthening the EMS workforce and integrating telehealth and home-based services through an EMS
system development and sustainability assessment, mobile integrated health care, and the High-risk Obstetrics (OB) and Neonatal 
Transport Project. Reduces unnecessary emergency room visits and targets rural Iowans by leveraging partnerships with local EMS 
agencies to improve maternal and neonatal outcomes.

Kansas
State Snapshot: 2026 Release

Kansas $225.90M
Budget Period 2 Funding
Initiative Name Initiative Description
Expand Primary and 
Secondary Prevention 
Programs
Achieves meaningful reductions in chronic disease and preventable hospitalizations for rural residents. This initiative deploys Community 
Health Workers (CHWs) and expands mobile cancer screenings to address the root causes of disease. The program leverages successful 
models like Food is Medicine (FIM) and Program of All-Inclusive Care for the Elderly (PACE), and partners with the Care Collaborative and 
Kansas State University to serve its target population.
Secure Local Access to 
Primary Care
Ensures rural Kansans have access to financially stable healthcare providers in their local communities. This initiative provides grants for 
regional partnerships, supports the conversion of facilities to rural emergency hospitals (REHs), and improves revenue cycles. It leverages 
partnerships with the Kansas Hospital Association and urban not-for-profit health systems to strengthen the state's rural delivery system.
Build a Sustainable Rural 
Health Workforce
Addresses provider shortages by creating a robust pipeline of healthcare professionals trained and incentivized to practice in rural 
Kansas. This initiative establishes rural physician residency programs and expands educational awards for students in high-demand allied 
health fields. The program partners with Kansas colleges and universities to offer career exploration programs and create a homegrown 
workforce.
Enable Value-Based Care
Transitions rural providers from a fee-for-service system to a value-based model that rewards quality outcomes and care coordination. 
This initiative offers financial incentives for adopting evidence-based practices and prepares providers to participate in Accountable Care 
Organizations (ACOs). The program leverages the state's Medicaid managed care organizations (MCOs) and a "shadow" ACO to support
providers in this transition.
Harness Data and 
T echnology
Empowers rural providers to improve patient care by expanding the use of telehealth, remote patient monitoring, and data analytics. This 
initiative provides telehealth navigators to coordinate specialty care and establishes a data infrastructure program to enhance providers' 
ability to use data effectively. It partners with the Care Collaborative to deploy nurses trained as telehealth navigators inprimary care 
practices.

Kentucky
State Snapshot: 2026 Release

Kentucky $211.33M
Budget Period 2 
Funding
Initiative Name Initiative Description
Chronic Care Innovations: 
Rural Community Hubs, 
Community Health Workers 
(CHWs), and Data and 
T echnology Strategy
Enhances chronic care innovation, starting with obesity and diabetes, through local community hubs designed to scale evidence-based 
interventions and explore digital health supports such as AI-enabled self-management coaching across the lifespan. Unites fragmented 
interventions, programs, resources, and technology tools for local community organizations to enable coordinated chronic disease
management. Rural Community Hubs are locally led at a regional level to align rural health stakeholders on shared priorities.Expands the 
CHW workforce through training opportunities. Enhances existing state-based resources for clinical care management and non-clinical 
resource coordination including closed loop referrals. Creates a Learning Collaborative to define desired data and technologycapabilities 
needed to support continuous learning to improve rural health.
PoWERing Rural Maternal 
and Infant Health: 
Community-based Teams
Enables timely initiation of quality, perinatal care from early pregnancy, postpartum and maternity care deserts through a network of 
coordinated telehealth-enabled maternal care teams. Establishes a scalable, patient-centered, and community-based model to address 
access to maternity and infant care in maternity care deserts. Leverages the PoWER (People Who Work for Engagement and Resilience) 
Team model with doulas, perinatal CHWs, and peer support specialists to provide essential services to mothers and infants during
pregnancy and the first year following the end of the pregnancy.
Rapid Response to 
Recovery: EmPATHModel, 
Mobile Crisis and T elehealth
Provides person-centered, technology-enabled access to integrated crisis care, linking EMS, behavioral health providers, and mobile crisis 
units through shared data and referral platforms. Builds on Kentucky's existing Emergency Psychiatric Assessment, Treatment and Healing 
(EmPATH) model and Mobile Crisis Intervention services. Creates an integrated, interoperable system that supports individualsthrough the 
behavioral health crisis continuum reducing system reliance on first responders. Utilizes telehealth technology and mobile crisis services 
to improve emergency response and ongoing patient recovery.
Rooted in Health: Rural 
Dental Access Program
Expands dental hygiene and dental assistant training, externship programs, and mobile/tele-dental hubs to address unmet oral health 
needs. Improves dental care in rural communities through comprehensive workforce development and expanded community access 
points, including advanced dental extraction training for rural dentists. Supports local providers by integrating tele-dentistry tools, 
establishing a statewide tele-dental network, and expanding mobile/portable dental care units. Reduces avoidable emergency department 
visits for non-emergent dental pain through proactive, community-based dental services.
$212.91M
Budget Period 1 
Funding

Kentucky
Initiative Name Initiative Description
From Crisis to Care: 
Integrated EMS and Trauma 
Response
Launches an EMS integrated care communication technology. Creates a registry of care management plans for children with complex 
medical needs to improve EMS response. Enhances prehospital capacity, enabling treat-no-transport and treat-in-place protocols and 
improved coordination with hospital trauma care. Empowers emergency medical professionals to safely treat patients in place using 
modern telemedicine tools. Supports the development of Community Paramedicine programs. Ensures smoother operational coordination 
with regional hospitals for trauma and specialized care through Regional Medical Operations Control Centers. Increases training 
opportunities for EMS workforce.
