Louisiana
State Snapshot: 2026 Release

Louisiana $211.86M
Budget Period 2 Funding
Initiative Name Initiative Description
Strengthen Health and 
Emergency Systems 
through Workforce 
Expansion and Integration
Addresses critical healthcare shortages and slow emergency response times in rural Louisiana through a three-part strategy. Expands the 
local workforce using financial incentives, tax credits, and educational partnerships to build a sustainable training pipeline. Enhances 
emergency response capacity in underserved parishes by launching community paramedicine and tele-EMS pilots.
Modernize T echnology 
Infrastructure and Capacity 
for Efficiency and Care 
Coordination
Integrates four key strategies to build a secure, interoperable health technology network using modern Application Programming 
Interfaces (APIs) and standardized data frameworks. Partnering with Federally Qualified Health Centers (FQHCs), schools, and libraries 
helps expand community digital literacy so all residents can participate. Collaborating with national partners like the National Academy 
for State Health Policy accelerates cross-state data sharing and public-private investment.
Reinforce Innovative, 
Outcomes-Based Care 
Delivery in Rural Areas
Launches risk-sharing and value-based payment arrangements to help rural hospitals, Accountable Care Organizations (ACOs), and 
FQHCs deliver better coordinated care. Supports high-impact, non-billable care models like care navigation and transitional services that 
reduce expensive emergency visits. Integrates these payment innovations into existing Medicaid and managed-care frameworks to create 
a sustainable, performance-driven reimbursement system.
Expand Physical Activity 
and Nutrition Interventions 
Through Community-Based 
Partnerships
Tackles high rates of food insecurity and chronic disease in rural areas by prioritizing preventive care and addressing non-clinical barriers. 
Employs a two-pronged approach that features direct nutrition interventions through food FARMacies alongside broad fitness programs. 
Integrates these community-based health models to sustainably improve overall population health and care coordination.
Louisiana is also implementing a Rural Technology Catalyst Fund, which promotes consumer-facing, technology-driven solutions for chronic disease 
prevention and management in rural populations.
$208.37M
Budget Period 1 
Funding

Louisiana
Initiative Name Initiative Description
Strengthen Care Integration 
for High-Needs Populations 
Through Coordinated, Multi-
Modal Models
Unites fragmented services into a cohesive, integrated care framework to support older adults and residents facing severe mental health 
challenges or diabetes. Combines physical, behavioral, and social support services to bridge critical care gaps in areas withsevere 
provider shortages. Delivers accessible, person-centered care closer to home to strengthen the full continuum of rural healthcare.
Strengthen Access to 
Essential Health Services 
Through Capital 
Investments
Modernizes rural healthcare facilities by funding thirty targeted capital investments to upgrade outdated medical equipment. Enables at 
least one facility in every rural parish to successfully transition from basic to advanced, tech-enabled care. Helps struggling hospitals 
overcome limited capital and high uninsured rates to better support the adoption of value-based payments.

B.2. Health and 
Lifestyle 
Rural populations are disproportionately impacted by chronic diseases. 
Promoting prevention through physical activity and proper nutrition can 
reduce overall cost of care burden and improve health outcomes. This 
State Policy Action captures whether a State requires schools to 
reestablish the Presidential Fitness Test.
At baseline, LA did not 
require schools to 
reestablish the Presidential 
Fitness Test 
LA committed to 
this State Policy 
Action
Complete $2.28M
B.3. SNAP 
Waivers
The Supplemental Nutrition Assistance Program (SNAP) provides food 
benefits to low-income families to supplement their grocery budget. 
Restricting the use of SNAP benefits on non -nutritious foods can help 
improve dietary intake and clinical indicators associated with long -term 
disease in rural populations. This State Policy Action captures State 
efforts to obtain a USDA SNAP Food Restriction Waiver to prohibit 
purchase of non-nutritious items.
At baseline, LA had a 
waiver approved by USDA
No State 
commitment (Wai
ver in place)
N/A $6.64M
B.4. Nutrition 
Continuing 
Medical 
Education
Improved nutrition education among physicians through continuing 
medical education (CME) requirements can directly contribute to 
improving the health of Americans who live in rural areas given the 
disproportionate impact of chronic disease on rural America. This State 
Policy Action captures State efforts to require nutrition to be a 
component for CME.
At baseline, LA had 
legislation requiring 
nutrition in CME for 
physicians
LA committed to 
this State Policy 
Action
Complete $5.38M

C.3. Certificate 
of Need
Certificate of Need (CON) laws require healthcare providers to 
seek permission from the State before they can open new facilities 
or expand service offerings. Eliminating or loosening CON laws 
allows providers to establish new facilities in underserved rural 
regions without increased burden, addressing the scarcity of local 
care options. This State Policy Action captures State efforts to 
ease CON laws across several service and facility types.
At baseline, LA had moderate CON 
regulations
No State 
Commitment N/A $1.44M
D.2. Licensure 
Compacts
Licensure compacts provide clinicians with the opportunity to 
serve patients across State borders, increasing the supply of 
accessible rural health providers. This State Policy Action 
captures State participation in licensure compacts for 
physicians, nursing, EMS, psychology, and Physician 
Assistants.
At baseline, LA was a member of 
three of the five licensure 
compacts
No State 
Commitment N/A $1.37M
D.3. Scope of 
Practice
Physician supply challenges in rural areas could be mitigated by 
expanding the scope of practice of other clinicians who have 
training and competency in caring for many of the cases currently 
limited to physician care. This State Policy Action captures State 
efforts to expand scope of practice for Physician Assistants, 
Nurse Practitioners, Pharmacists, and Dental Hygienists.
At baseline, LA had moderate or 
reduced scope of practice (SOP) 
for PAs and NPs. Pharmacist and 
Dental Hygienist SOP were 
considered restricted
No State 
Commitment N/A $0.79M

E.3. Short-term, 
limited-duration 
insurance 
(STLDI)
Rural populations consistently have higher uninsurance rates than 
their urban counterparts. Short -term, limited duration insurance 
(STLDI) plans may offer some rural individuals lower -cost, short-
term coverage options. This State Policy Action captures 
whether a State restricts availability, use, or timeframe of STLDI 
beyond the latest federal guidance.
At baseline, LA did not restrict 
STLDI plans beyond the latest 
federal guidance
No State 
commitment (No 
STLDI plan 
restrictions 
beyond federal 
guidance)
N/A $2.57M

Care Services

clinicians to provide care to rural residents from another location 
via telehealth, remote patient monitoring, or other modalities. This 
State Policy Action captures State Medicaid coverage of remote 
care services and the presence of other policies to allow out -of-
State clinicians to provide telehealth/remote care.

two types of remote care services 
and had policies in place to allow

Maine
State Snapshot: 2026 Release

Maine $190.36M
Budget Period 2 
Funding
Initiative Name Initiative Description
Population Health: 
Promoting Timely Access T o 
High-Quality Care
Promotes timely access to high-quality care for rural Mainers facing complex health challenges, including aging residents and those with 
chronic conditions. This initiative expands mobile services, establishes more School Based Health Centers (SBHCs), and improves access 
to substance use disorder treatments. The plan requires collaboration with schools, healthcare providers, and Tribal governments to better 
coordinate care.
Workforce: Strengthening 
Maine's Rural Health 
Workforce
Strengthens Maine's rural health workforce to address provider shortages that limit access to care. It aims to grow the pipeline of health 
workers through new training programs and offers financial incentives to attract and retain clinicians in rural communities. The initiative 
partners with health employers and state education systems to establish successful career exploration models and build K-12 talent 
pipelines. Provides customized training through the Healthcare Training for ME (HTFM), which includes a centralized portal for health 
employers to share their training needs and access workforce resources, rural training labs, and mobile simulation units.
T echnology: Modernizing 
Rural Care Delivery With 
Digital Health T echnology
Modernizes rural care delivery by enabling providers and patients to benefit from technology and information advances. This effort expands 
access to telehealth, upgrades electronic medical record systems, and supports the adoption of artificial intelligence applications. 
Telehealth activities include a virtual quick (urgent) care program, tele-behavioral health (Tele-BH) program targeted to youth and perinatal 
care, on-demand specialty telehealth services for special populations, and provider-to-provider specialty on-demand (synchronous) 
consultations.
Access: Bridging The 
Healthcare Affordability Gap 
For Rural Mainers
Addresses financial and transportation barriers to healthcare experienced by rural Mainers. It includes providing payments toreimburse for 
uncompensated care and using technology to help residents select the best value health coverage. The initiative will also improve 
coordination of existing transportation solutions.
Sustainable Rural Health 
Ecosystems: Addressing 
Financial Instability Of Rural 
Providers
Supports the long-term financial strength and resilience of Maine's rural health ecosystem. Provides tailored support to hospitals facing 
financial challenges, helps develop regional structures for improved payment models, and expands the service continuum. Fundscapital 
improvements and technology to help rural providers strengthen their financial stability. Develops regional structures to support improved 
alternative payment models (APMs) through capital investments and technical assistance.

Maryland
State Snapshot: 2026 Release

Maryland
Initiative Name Initiative Description
Transform the Rural Health 
Workforce
Develops a strong health workforce in rural communities by expanding existing efforts and implementing new ones to recruit and retain 
healthcare clinicians and allied health professionals. It leverages Maryland's existing Registered Apprenticeship program andArea Health 
Education Centers (AHECs), and requires key partnerships with the Maryland Department of Labor, the state School of Medicine,and 
healthcare providers. 
Promote Sustainable Access 
and Innovative Care for Rural 
Marylanders
Expands access to healthcare services for rural Marylanders by supporting the growth of primary and specialty care practices in rural 
communities. The initiative focuses on increasing primary and behavioral health capacity, improving the efficiency of local health agencies, 
and strengthening emergency trauma response. It builds upon Maryland's experience with value-based care models and involves key 
partnerships with the Maryland Health Care Commission (MHCC), Health Services Cost Review Commission (HSCRC), local health 
departments, school-based health centers, the Maryland Institute for Emergency Medical Services Systems (MIEMSS), and the state’s 
designated health information exchange.
Empower Rural Marylanders 
to Eat for Health
Improves access to nutritious, locally grown foods for rural Marylanders by investing in infrastructure and creating demand for healthy foods. 
This initiative will expand cold storage facilities, support small grocers and mobile markets, and provide nutrition education to residents in 
rural areas. The program builds and leverages grant programming and will be executed through partnerships with the Maryland Department of 
Agriculture, the Maryland Department of Housing and Community Development, Maryland Department of Emergency Management, Rural
Maryland Council, and local health departments.

Massachusetts
State Snapshot: 2026 Release

Massachusetts $158.39M
Budget Period 2 
Funding
Initiative Name Initiative Description
Population Health 
Advancement
Improves clinical management of chronic disease through enhanced clinical and community coordination. This initiative expands
participation in value-based payment models and creates stronger linkages between rural providers. The program will advance rural 
population health, improve quality of care, and increase efficiencies to lower healthcare costs.
Innovation in Rural Care 
Models
Advances rural-appropriate care models by standing up mobile health units and expanding telehealth services. This initiative supports the 
expansion of proven models and integrated specialty care networks to increase access for rural residents. Program investmentswill provide 
technical assistance and fund the infrastructure required to broaden service availability across these communities.
Training Healthcare for 
Retention, Innovation, and 
Excellence (THRIVE) 
Strengthens the full continuum of the healthcare workforce in rural communities with targeted activities focused on workforce
development, recruitment, and retention. This initiative will focus on programs that will attract, train, and support healthcare providers in 
rural areas. The programs are designed to encourage providers to commit to serving their rural communities for a minimum of 5years and 
thrive professionally.
Healthy Rural Communities
Empowers rural populations by expanding local organizations to deliver community-based chronic disease prevention. This initiative 
addresses the root causes of poor health by supporting proven, tailored educational programs. It leverages community networksto create 
critical linkages between these local prevention efforts and clinical care providers.
Emergency Medical System 
(EMS) Service Integration
Strengthens the viability and clinical role of EMS for rural communities. This initiative deploys a new prehospital protocol and medication 
dosing mobile application to improve the quality of care before patients reach the hospital. This initiative will support integrating new EMS 
services in rural areas to increase their role in providing clinical care. It will support healthcare efficiencies and new reimbursement 
mechanisms for ambulances while ultimately providing more operational stability to these local agencies and systems that provide critical 
services.
Massachusetts is also implementing a Rural Technology Catalyst Fund, which promotes consumer-facing, technology-driven solutions for chronic disease 
prevention and management in rural populations.
$162.01M
Budget Period 1 
Funding

Massachusetts
Initiative Name Initiative Description
Enhancing T echnology 
Interoperability and 
Connectivity
Improves the technological infrastructure of rural health providers to increase connectivity, create efficiencies, and support better 
outcomes. This initiative will coordinate projects designed to modernize rural health information technology (IT) systems andenhance 
secure data exchange. It will promote statewide connectivity to better support rural areas and providers.
Facility Modernization and 
Re-Use
Supports renovations and equipment upgrades of rural facilities to optimize space and expand access to health services for residents of 
rural areas. This initiative will improve operational efficiency and increase local access to services. Renovations of existing facilities will 
increase facility services and capacity, improve patient safety and quality of care, and reduce unused space.

Michigan
State Snapshot: 2026 Release

Michigan $170.61M
Budget Period 2 
Funding
Initiative Name Initiative Description
Transforming Rural Health 
through Partnerships 
Initiative 
Reimagines Michigan’s rural health landscape by piloting and scaling community oriented approaches to care leveraging Hubs that serve 
as points of collaboration to address health and social outcomes. The Hub will connect local, community and regional partnersutilizing 
Community Health Workers (CHWs), Community Information Exchange (CIE), Health Information Exchange (HIE), and Health in All 
Policies (HiAP) approaches to address root causes of disease. 
Workforce for Wellness
Strengthens the rural health and wellness workforce through training, recruitment, and retention programs within rural Michigan. The 
initiative provides training for professionals including behavioral health, maternal health, EMS, primary care, and other high need 
specialists. Pipeline programs, stipends, and educational awards will support current workers in rural communities while increasing the 
number of health professionals. 
Interoperability in Action
Enhances the technical infrastructure and interoperability of health data in Michigan’s rural areas by investing in electronic health record 
(EHR systems, clinical and administrative technology, HIE and CIE capability. Pilot programs will link EMS data with EHR systems to 
patient improve care coordination. At the onset, the initiative will connect partners from various sectors including rural providers, and IT 
experts to develop statewide standards for data sharing and integration. 
Care Closer to Home 
Blueprint
Creates sustainable, community-based pathways that bring health services closer to where people live and work. By promoting 
innovations that improve chronic disease management, maternal and child health, and behavioral health outcomes, the initiative aims to 
make care more efficient, coordinated, and person-centered. The initiative will connect disparate partners within health and social 
sectors.

Minnesota
State Snapshot: 2026 Release

Minnesota $193.83M
Budget Period 2 
Funding
Initiative Name Initiative Description
Community-Based 
Preventive Care and 
Chronic Disease 
Management 
Supports rural hospitals, clinics, and Tribal health organizations in providing preventive care for chronic diseases to Minnesotans living in 
rural areas. This initiative increases access to screenings and self-management tools by building on existing programs like the Minnesota 
2035 Plan and community care hubs. Executing this initiative requires collaboration with local public health departments, community 
organizations, and technical assistance providers.
Recruit and Retain Talent in 
Rural Communities 
Addresses workforce shortages by creating pathways for students and incumbent workers to enter and advance in rural healthcare 
careers. This initiative targets high school students and existing rural workers, leveraging "Earn and Train" apprenticeship models and 
expanding rural clinical rotations. Key partnerships include Minnesota State Colleges and Universities, rural hospitals, clinics, and the 
University of Minnesota Medical School.
Sustain Access to Services 
to Keep Care Closer to 
Home 
Encompasses technology-focused solutions, mobile care delivery, and integration of underused, community-embedded frontline health 
care team members such as community health workers (CHWs), community health representatives (CHRs), doulas, peer support 
specialists, and community paramedics in community and clinical settings in a financially sustainable way. The initiative focuses on rural 
residents who face barriers to care by expanding telehealth access points and mobile physical and oral health units. 
Create Regional Care 
Models to Improve Whole 
Person Health 
Supports collaborative regional activities to improve access to timely and appropriate health care, with a focus on essentialobstetrics 
and mental health services. Develops provider-to-provider telehealth linkages that provide access to professional services not available in 
the immediate community and connect primary care providers to specialist expertise when needed. Establishes provider-to-provider
learning and support networks using simulation training for obstetrics and Extension for Community Healthcare Outcomes (ECHO)
programs to improve clinical knowledge, reduce professional isolation, and strengthen the quality of care in underserved regions, keeping 
patients close to home. 
$193.09M
Budget Period 1 
Funding

Minnesota
Initiative Name Initiative Description
Invest in T echnology, 
Infrastructure, and 
Collaboration for Financial 
Viability 
Strengthens the financial health of rural providers by supporting investments in technology and data infrastructure. The initiative assists 
rural hospitals, clinics, and Tribal Nations that struggle with cost-based reimbursement and low patient volumes. Supports investment in 
a secure statewide integrated rural health data network to support real-time clinical and referral data sharing across health care, public 
health, and community settings. Allows rural provider data for population health and quality improvement measures, ensuring rural 
providers can fully participate in statewide quality improvement initiatives and alternative payment arrangements while maintaining 
efficient, secure workflows.

C.3. Certificate 
of Need
Certificate of Need (CON) laws require healthcare providers to seek 
permission from the State before they can open new facilities or expand 
service offerings. Eliminating or loosening CON laws allows providers to 
establish new facilities in underserved rural regions without increased 
burden, addressing the scarcity of local care options. This State Policy 
Action captures State efforts to ease CON laws across several service 
and facility types.
At baseline, MN had 
limited CON regulations
No State 
commitment N/A $2.16M

Compacts

across State borders, increasing the supply of accessible rural health 
providers. This State Policy Action captures State participation in 
licensure compacts for physicians, nursing, EMS, psychology, and 
Physician Assistants.
At baseline, MN was a 
member of three of the 
five licensure compacts

commitment N/A $1.60M

Practice

for Physician Assistants, Nurse Practitioners, Pharmacists, and Dental 
Hygienists.

advanced or full scope of 
practice (SOP) for PAs,

SOP was considered 
limited authority

Mississippi
State Snapshot: 2026 Release

Mississippi $204.68M
Budget Period 2 
Funding
Initiative Name Initiative Description
Statewide Rural Health 
Assessment 
Conducts a comprehensive assessment of rural health needs to confirm and improve the state's existing plan. Engages ≥150 stakeholders 
across hospitals, Federally Qualified Health Centers (FQHCs), EMS, behavioral health, and community representatives. Identifies ≥5 
major service, workforce, or infrastructure gaps statewide and deliver a data-driven roadmap with recommendations aligned with 
assessment findings, prioritized by urgency, impact, and feasibility.
Coordinated Regional 
Integrated Systems (CRIS) 
Initiative 
Strengthens regional care networks and patient care continuity reduces low-acuity ER visits through nurse navigation and treat-in-place 
interventions. Engages rural hospitals and clinics in coordinated care networks, and ensures high-risk patients have completed care plans 
within 7 days of discharge, resulting in lower 30-day readmission rates for high-risk patients. 
Workforce Expansion 
Initiative (WEI)
Strengthens the rural healthcare workforce to improve access and quality of care for residents. The program focuses on recruiting, 
training, and retaining a wide range of healthcare professionals in rural areas. It builds a sustainable workforce by formingpartnerships 
with local schools, community colleges, and vocational programs.
Health Technology 
Advancement and 
Modernization (HTAM) 
Initiative 
Modernizes rural healthcare systems by improving the technology that supports quality care. Supports digital health by upgrading
providers’ interoperable EHR systems, enabling eligible providers to actively share data through the statewide health information 
exchange Health Information Exchange (HIE). Provides applicable provider staff with cybersecurity or IT training, and patients with digital 
tools, including patient portals, telehealth, and remote monitoring.
$205.91M
Budget Period 1 
Funding

Mississippi
Initiative Name Initiative Description
T elehealth Adoption and 
Provider Support (TAPS) 
Initiative 
Increases virtual care access and capacity by enrolling and sustain eligible providers in telehealth service delivery for at least two 
consecutive years. Provides applicable provider staff will complete telehealth competency training within the first year.
Building Rural 
Infrastructure for Delivery, 
Growth, and Efficiency 
(BRIDGE) Initiative 
Improves rural behavioral health access and infrastructure by having ≥3 Psychiatric Emergency Services (PES) units established, and ≥100 
Community Health Workers and clinical staff trained and deployed. Supports completion of ≥25 rural capital projects, contributing to 
≥15% increases in service capacity, a ≥15% reduction in patient travel distances, and a ≥15% reduction in preventable emergency room 
(ER) visits in pilot program areas. ≥50% of pilot programs will show measurable improvements, and BRIDGE will reach ≥100,000 rural 
residents statewide.

Missouri
State Snapshot: 2026 Release

Missouri $218.55M
Budget Period 2 
Funding
Initiative Name Initiative Description
Regional Coordinating 
Networks and Hub 
Activation
Establishes a network of 30 Local Community Hubs and seven Regional Coordinating Networks to improve care coordination for rural
Missourians. This initiative builds on the state's Transformation of Rural Community Health Care (ToRCHCare) pilot program to improve 
access and health outcomes for residents across 104 rural counties by incentivizing organizations within the Hubs and Networks to 
continue collaboration. 
Alternative Payment Models
Develops new payment models that reward rural providers for delivering high-quality, high-value care instead of just the volume of services. 
This program targets all rural providers and payers in Missouri, aiming to improve financial stability for a system where over half of rural 
hospitals operate at a loss. The initiative will leverage Missouri's Medicaid program and engage with commercial payers and Medicare 
Advantage plans to create sustainable financial incentives.
Digital Backbone
Creates a unified digital infrastructure to connect Missouri's fragmented rural health systems and enable secure data sharingamong 
providers. This initiative targets all rural providers, including hospitals, clinics, and pharmacies, by establishing a RuralHealth Data 
Collaborative and promoting interoperability. It builds on existing platforms including the Hospital Industry Data Institute (HIDI) and 
requires partnerships with EHR vendors, technology companies, and various state health departments.
Rural Health Workforce 
Programs
Builds a sustainable pipeline of healthcare professionals to serve in rural Missouri through coordinated recruitment, training, and retention 
efforts. The program targets students and existing healthcare workers expanding career education in high schools, launching maternal 
health workforce expansion programs, establishing more clerkship opportunities, and supporting EMS workforce development. This 
involves partnerships with the Department of Higher Education & Workforce Development, Missouri's medical schools, and local 
healthcare providers, and provides childcare access and housing supports for rural healthcare workers.
Provider Transformation
Strengthens the operational and technological capabilities of rural healthcare providers to ensure their long-term stability and improve 
patient care. This initiative focuses on rural providers needing to modernize tools and facilities, targeting those at risk of closure due to 
financial strain. It will be implemented through partnerships with Regional Coordinating Networks and Community Hubs to deploy 
innovations like Remote Patient Monitoring and Ambient AI for documentation.

Montana $239.74M

Funding
Initiative Name Initiative Description
Develop Workforce Through 
Recruitment, Training, and 
Retention
Combats Montana's growing healthcare workforce gap by implementing a coordinated set of recruitment, training, and retention actions in 
rural areas. Collaborates closely with the Montana Department of Labor and Industry to strengthen the state's broader workforce pipeline. 
Leverages and expands upon the existing framework of the state’s workforce Initiative to support local clinical professionals.
Ensure Rural Facility 
Sustainability and Access 
Through Partnership and 
Restructuring
Leads a statewide care delivery transformation to help rural health systems right-size services and restructure operational costs. Optimizes 
population health clinical infrastructure to sustainably meet the unique needs of local communities providing essential time-sensitive care 
for acute incidents like stroke. Partners with rural providers and utilizes remote care services to protect and expand healthcare access.
Launch innovative Care 
Delivery and Payment 
Models
Transforms care delivery and financing to incentivize high-quality healthcare throughout rural Montana. Accelerates primary care
participation in Medicaid provider payment incentives while establishing integrated programs for dually eligible Medicare andMedicaid 
beneficiaries. Strengthens emergency medical services and community care through strategic rural partnerships.
Invest in Community Health 
and Preventive 
Infrastructure
Addresses critical care gaps through strategic investments in community-oriented healthcare systems including school-based care,mobile 
care vans, and workforce programs within tribal communities. Builds out strong population health clinical infrastructure to support 
underserved rural residents. Promotes positive health and lifestyle changes to reduce the long-term burden of chronic disease.
Deploy Modern Health Care 
T echnologies to Guide Rural 
Health Interventions
Advances technological innovation to improve care coordination and enhance overall population health outcomes in rural areas.
Establishes data-driven clinical interventions for efficient provider operations and care delivery to rural communities. Deploysmodern tools 
to expand remote care capabilities for patients living in geographically isolated regions.

Nebraska
State Snapshot: 2026 Release

Nebraska $219.25M
Budget Period 2 
Funding
Initiative Name Initiative Description
Make Rural Nebraska 
Healthy Again Through Food 
as Medicine
Lowers obesity risk and prevents chronic diseases for rural Nebraskans by improving access to healthy foods and nutrition education. This 
initiative supports rural schools in transitioning to whole fresh foods through grants for kitchen modernization and establishing physical 
activity and wellness programs. It also leverages partnerships with the agriculture sector and educational institutions to promote healthy 
eating from a young age.
Regionalized Rural Access 
and Navigation
Builds a statewide system of regionalized care that ensures every rural resident can access the right services at the right time. This initiative 
addresses challenges in rural healthcare by coordinating emergency medical and perinatal care and embedding community health workers 
into local health departments. The program leverages partnerships with the Department of Veterans Affairs and local health organizations 
to improve access for all rural residents.
Rural Workforce 
Acceleration
Recruits, trains, and retains a resilient healthcare workforce to serve rural and frontier communities in Nebraska. The initiative provides 
financial incentives for providers who commit to practicing in rural areas and uses a virtual reality simulation network to offer advanced 
training. It establishes career pathways for Supplemental Nutrition Assistance Program (SNAP) recipients to enter the healthcare field and 
introduces school-age students to medical careers.
eHealth and Mobile
Brings high-quality, technology-enabled healthcare to rural and frontier Nebraskans through mobile, remote, and eHealth solutions. This 
program deploys mobile clinics to provide maternal and infant care directly within maternity care deserts and brings urgent dental care to 
rural areas. It also helps community pharmacies use digital tools to manage chronic diseases and provides remote patient monitoring for 
individuals in their homes.
Rural Emergency Behavioral 
Health
Creates an integrated, community-based emergency behavioral health system to improve outcomes and reduce costs. The initiative 
partners with the Nebraska Medical Association to co-locate mental health professionals in rural primary care clinics and implement a 
behavioral health nursing home pilot. It also leverages telehealth technology to give law enforcement instant access to crisis professionals 
and retrofits existing facilities to increase crisis stabilization capacity.
$218.53M
Budget Period 1 
Funding

Nebraska
Initiative Name Initiative Description
Assisted Living Facility (ALF) 
Special Needs Population 
Incentive Model
Strengthens Nebraska’s long-term care system and increases access to specialized services for medically complex patients in rural 
counties. The program provides incentive payments to facilities that offer memory care and helps fund facility modernizationsto safely 
accommodate residents with high-acuity needs. It targets individuals who are dual-eligible or aged 65 and older with dementia oradults 
with other complex medical conditions.
Nebraska Rural Health 
T echnology Catalyst Fund 
and Partnership Initiative 
(NETECH)
Drives technology-driven transformation in rural healthcare by investing in innovative startups and scalable solutions. The fundidentifies 
and supports technologies that improve access to care, enhance disease management, and promote healthy living. This initiative convenes 
investors, health systems, and tribal partners to create an investment pipeline that multiplies public funding with private-sector co-
investment.
Nebraska is also implementing a Rural Technology Catalyst Fund, which promotes consumer-facing, technology-driven solutions for chronic disease 
prevention and management in rural populations.

C.3. Certificate 
of Need
Certificate of Need (CON) laws require healthcare providers to seek 
permission from the State before they can open new facilities or expand 
service offerings. Eliminating or loosening CON laws allows providers to 
establish new facilities in underserved rural regions without increased 
burden, addressing the scarcity of local care options. This State Policy 
Action captures State efforts to ease CON laws across several service 
and facility types.

CON regulations

Nevada
State Snapshot: 2026 Release

Nevada $182.40M
Budget Period 2 
Funding
Initiative Name Initiative Description
Rural Health Outcomes 
Accelerator Program 
Funds evidence-based initiatives to improve health outcomes for rural residents with chronic disease, behavioral health needs, and 
maternal or infant health challenges. This initiative expands value-based payment models and integrates community health workersinto 
coordinated care teams to improve patient outcomes. The program leverages a Rural Value Acceleration Network (RVAN) and 
partnerships with rural hospitals, clinics, and academic institutions.
Nevada Rural Health 
System Flex Fund
Modernizes and enhances existing facilities and infrastructure for Nevada's rural healthcare system. This initiative providesflexible 
funding for capital expenditures at rural clinics, hospitals, and EMS agencies to improve the quality, capacity, and sustainability of local 
care. The program encourages regional purchasing arrangements and partnerships with larger health systems to support self-sustaining 
models for delivering essential services.
Workforce Recruitment & 
Rural Access Program 
(WRRAP)
Attracts and retains physicians and other providers in rural areas through specific retention strategies and incentive programs under the 
WRRAP . This initiative builds a "Grow-Your-Own" pipeline by creating career pathways from high school and supporting local training 
programs and rural residencies. The program partners with rural hospitals, clinics, and academic institutions to address long-term 
workforce shortages.
Rural Health Innovation & 
T echnology (RHIT) Grant
Creates a more connected and secure system of care by funding new tech-enabled hybrid care solutions and remote patient monitoring. 
This initiative helps build the necessary infrastructure to expand the use of innovative telehealth services and supports front-line providers 
caring for patients with complex needs. It will prioritize technology solutions that are sustainable and operationally efficient in low-
bandwidth environments for Nevada's rural communities.

New Hampshire
State Snapshot: 2026 Release

New Hampshire $204.31M
Budget Period 2 
Funding
Initiative Name Initiative Description
Population Health
Invests in health infrastructure, care coordination, and community outreach to strengthen prevention-first care for rural residents. 
Replicates community-based nursing, mobile integrated health, and community health worker models. Launches the state's first Dual 
Eligible Special Needs Plan to better integrate care for individuals enrolled in both Medicaid and Medicare.
Healthcare Access
Expands evidence-based care coordination and disease management models to improve medical outcomes for rural New Hampshire 
residents. Increases access to primary, behavioral, oral, perinatal, and specialty care by using telehealth technologies. Expects to 
successfully raise the total number of behavioral health, dental, pharmacy, and community-based visits.
Workforce
Strengthens the rural healthcare workforce through a coordinated partnership between local high school career technical education 
programs, post-secondary institutions, and local healthcare providers. Creates clear career pathways from high school to graduate 
degrees, including a new family medicine residency program in Coos County. Addresses critical workforce retention issues by expanding 
training opportunities and improving access to local childcare.
T echnology Modernizes care delivery across rural communities by deploying scalable, integrated technology solutions. Introduces artificial 
intelligence tools to streamline clinical decisions, real-time claims, and administrative authorizations.
Financial Sustainability
Strengthens the financial sustainability of the rural healthcare system by investing heavily in prevention-first and value-based care. 
Prepares local healthcare providers to transition into value-based and two-sided risk arrangements. Integrates clinical capacityand 
technical infrastructure to reduce hospital discharge barriers and maintain critical safety-net providers.

Initiative Name Initiative Description
NJ RHT 1 - Workforce: 
Building the workforce we 
need to care for rural New 
Jerseyans
Supports the recruitment, training, and retention of a highly skilled clinical and non-clinical healthcare workforce to serve rural residents. 
Trains and deploys trusted community extenders such as community health workers and doulas to expand access to care. Expands upon 
existing reflective supervision activities to support workforce retention.
NJ RHT 2 - Infrastructure: 
T argeted infrastructure 
investments to transform 
care for rural New Jerseyans
Strengthens stability and capacity in the rural healthcare ecosystem with targeted investment in essential providers. Prioritizes primary 
care providers (including Federally Qualified Health Centers), and hospitals. Makes dedicated investments in rural trauma andemergency 
services.
NJ RHT 3 - T echnology: 
Investing in technologies 
that bring care to where 
people live
Delivers innovative health services and mobile care solutions directly into rural communities outside of traditional office settings. 
Collaborates with non-profits, health systems and tech industry partners to ensure the efficient, impactful, and responsible adoption of 
new technologies. 
NJ RHT 4 - Prevention: 
Focusing on preventive 
health interventions for 
rural New Jerseyans
Strengthens rural health systems by investing in evidence-based prevention activities to maximize long-term health improvements.
Supports community organizations, healthcare providers, and public health partners to expand outreach, education, and access.
Expands the operational capacity of EMS.
NJ RHT 5 - Chronic disease: 
Helping rural New 
Jerseyans improve chronic 
disease outcomes
Strengthens chronic disease prevention, management, and wellness in rural areas. Supports community partnerships across healthcare 
providers, community groups, and faith-based organizations to expand healthy lifestyle and nutrition programs.

in Year 2
B.3. SNAP 
Waivers
The Supplemental Nutrition Assistance Program (SNAP) provides food 
benefits to low-income families to supplement their grocery budget. 
Restricting the use of SNAP benefits on non -nutritious foods can help 
improve dietary intake and clinical indicators associated with long -term 
disease in rural populations. This State Policy Action captures State 
efforts to obtain a USDA SNAP Food Restriction Waiver to prohibit 
purchase of non-nutritious items.
At baseline, NJ had active 
legislation to create 
SNAP waiver
No State 
commitment N/A $1.66M
C.3. Certificate 
of Need
Certificate of Need (CON) laws require healthcare providers to seek 
permission from the State before they can open new facilities or expand 
service offerings. Eliminating or loosening CON laws allows providers to 
establish new facilities in underserved rural regions without increased 
burden, addressing the scarcity of local care options. This State Policy 
Action captures State efforts to ease CON laws across several service 
and facility types.
At baseline, NJ had 
limited CON regulations
No State 
commitment N/A $2.16M

D.2. Licensure 
Compacts
Licensure compacts provide clinicians with the opportunity to serve patients 
across State borders, increasing the supply of accessible rural health 
providers. This State Policy Action captures State participation in 
licensure compacts for physicians, nursing, EMS, psychology, and 
Physician Assistants.

member of three of the 
five licensure compacts
No State 
commitment N/A $1.60M
D.3. Scope of 
Practice
Physician supply challenges in rural areas could be mitigated by expanding 
the scope of practice of other clinicians who have training and competency 
in caring for many of the cases currently limited to physician care. This 
State Policy Action captures State efforts to expand scope of practice 
for Physician Assistants, Nurse Practitioners, Pharmacists, and Dental 
Hygienists.
At baseline, NJ had 
moderate scope of 
practice (SOP) for PAs, 
reduced SOP for NPs, 
restricted SOP for 
Pharmacists, and semi -
restricted SOP for Dental 
Hygienists
No State 
commitment N/A $1.19M
F .1. Remote 
Care Services
Remote care services can help expand access to care by allowing clinicians 
to provide care to rural residents from another location via telehealth, 
remote patient monitoring, or other modalities. This State Policy Action 
captures State Medicaid coverage of remote care services and the 
presence of other policies to allow out -of-State clinicians to provide 
telehealth/remote care.
At baseline, NJ Medicaid 
covered all three types of 
remote care services and 
had policies in place to 
allow out-of-State 
clinicians to provide 
telehealth/remote care

(Medicaid 
coverage for 
specified remote 
care services in

remote care 
access policies in

New Mexico
State Snapshot: 2026 Release

New Mexico $211.04M
Budget Period 2 
Funding
Initiative Name Initiative Description
Healthy Horizons: 
Expanding Specialty Care 
Access and Chronic Disease 
Management
Expands specialty care for rural and Tribal communities by using a combination of in-person visits, e-consults, and remote health tools. 
This approach strengthens chronic disease management for high-risk populations by improving prevention and integrating remote care 
solutions. Integrates behavioral health within chronic and maternal care networks, ensuring whole-person care across all rural and Tribal 
communities. Keeps care close to home and reduces unnecessary travel and delays in specialty care. Supports use of remote care tools 
(e.g., e-consults, telehealth, remote patient monitoring (RPM)) to expand access, support continuity, and reduce long travel times. 
Rooted in New Mexico: 
Building T omorrow's Rural 
Health Workforce
Strengthens the recruitment, training, and retention of health professionals to ensure care remains local for rural, frontier, and Tribal 
communities. It builds on successful programs like FORWARD NM and Project ECHO (Extension for Community Healthcare Outcomes) to 
address workforce shortages and high turnover . The initiative fosters local interest in health careers by launching K-12 pathway programs 
and offering incentives for service commitments.
Rural Health Innovation 
Fund: Enabling Community-
Designed, Community-Led 
Change
Establishes a grant program to support community-designed and community-led initiatives that respect and reflect the unique practices 
and priorities of different pueblos, Tribal nations, and rural communities. It provides opportunities for rural, frontier, and Tribal communities 
to address specific challenges that require local solutions. This fund allows providers and community-based organizations to apply for 
grants to address issues such as food insecurity, transportation, and preventive care.
Bridge to Resilience: Rural 
Health Sustainability & 
Innovation Center (RHSIC)
Supports financially distressed rural providers with tools and knowledge to operate more effectively and achieve sustainability. It delivers 
education and learning collaboratives focused on workforce recruitment, financial stability, and digital tools. This initiative helps providers 
by establishing new processes and training staff to carry best practices forward independently. RHSIC directly supports ruralproviders by 
coordinating and delivering high-impact technical assistance (e.g., operations, finance, service optimization, regional partnership 
coordination).
Rural Health Data Hub: 
Establishing a Health 
Analytics Platform
Builds a secure data infrastructure to enhance analytic capabilities, improve predictive analytics, and increase health data transparency 
across the state. Integrates patient information across medical, behavioral, and social services to promote a unified care experience for 
rural residents. The platform makes data available to consumers and providers to inform decision-making at the local, regional, and state 
levels.

New York
State Snapshot: 2026 Release

New York $207.75M
Budget Period 2 
Funding
Initiative Name Initiative Description
Rural Community Health 
Integration
Strengthens the rural health safety net by building integrated health networks to enhance care delivery and coordination. This initiative 
encourages collaborative partnerships between rural hospitals, community providers, and other healthcare organizations. The program 
leverages the existing infrastructure of anchor institutions (institutions with demonstrated capacity and commitment in serving their 
constituents) to establish a path toward financial sustainability for all participating facilities.
Strengthening Rural 
Communities with 
T echnology-Enhanced 
Primary Care
Leverages historic investments in primary care by expanding the number of Patient-Centered Medical Home (PCMH)-recognized providers. 
This initiative helps rural practices adopt technology and advanced analytics to improve operational efficiency, quality, andfinancial 
sustainability. The program utilizes a cohort-based model to guide practices through PCMH recognition and implementation of technology-
enabled workflows.
Rural Roots: Building a 
Sustainable Rural Health 
care Workforce
Addresses health care staffing shortages in rural communities by growing local talent and recruiting health professionals through multi-year 
service commitments. This initiative introduces students in grades 6-12 to health care careers and expands training opportunities at rural 
facilities. The program will build a data system that tracks health care workforce trends, needed specialties, and existing gaps in each rural 
county.
Investments in T ech 
Innovation & Cybersecurity 
Enhancements for Rural NY 
Advances rural health care in New York by investing in innovative technologies and robust cybersecurity measures. This initiative deploys 
innovative digital health tools and platforms that streamline care delivery, enhance patient access, and support data-driven decision-
making. The program focuses on fortifying the cybersecurity posture of rural health care facilities through advanced endpointprotection 
and comprehensive staff training.

North Carolina
State Snapshot: 2026 Release

North Carolina $216.82M
Budget Period 2 
Funding
Initiative Name Initiative Description
Build Rural Community Care 
Network “Hubs”
Establishes locally governed networks called North Carolina Rural Organizations Orchestrating Transformation for Sustainability (NC 
ROOTS) Hubs to coordinate health services for rural residents. The hubs will connect people to medical, behavioral, and social supports, 
building on lessons learned from NC’s implementation of previous hub and spoke models. This initiative requires partnerships between the 
North Carolina Department of Health and Human Services (NCDHHS), healthcare providers, and community organizations.
Create Models & Capacity 
for Expanded Primary Care, 
Prevention, and Chronic 
Disease Management
Improves access to primary and preventive care for rural populations focused on chronic diseases and perinatal health. It expands 
successful programs like the 'I Gave Birth' model and coordinates with partners to manage conditions and improve nutrition. This effort will 
use NC ROOTS Hubs to connect families with community partners and fresh food resources in line with Food is Medicine efforts.
Expand and Integrate 
Behavioral Health and 
Substance Use Disorder 
Services
Expands access to mental health and substance use services in rural areas by building on the state's Certified Community Behavioral 
Health Clinics (CCBHCs) model and coordinating efforts with regional ROOTS Hubs. This effort targets youth and adults by integrating care, 
launching mobile crisis teams, and using telehealth. Key partners include local health departments and community health centers 
implementing school-based services and various community organizations to improve care coordination.
Build a Robust & Resilient 
Workforce & Innovative Care 
T eam Models for Rural 
Communities
Strengthens the rural healthcare workforce by creating regional training hubs and expanding development programs. It focuses on training 
and retaining providers like social workers, doulas, and community health workers in rural communities. The initiative will partner with the 
NC ROOTS hubs, NC Community College System and universities to offer training and tuition support.
Ensure Fiscal Sustainability 
of Rural Health Providers 
Through Innovative Financial 
Models
Supports the financial health of rural hospitals and clinics by moving from payment models based on volume to ones based on value. It 
provides technical help and funding for providers to adopt these new models, building on NC Medicaid’s prior investment in population 
health and value- based payment. This will be done through the NC ROOTS Hubs with primary care providers and hospitals.
$213.01M
Budget Period 1 
Funding

North Carolina
Initiative Name Initiative Description
Modernize Rural Care 
Delivery Through Digital 
Forward Solutions
Upgrades technology in rural healthcare to improve patient care and provider efficiency. It establishes a Rural Health Innovation Fund to 
help providers adopt new tools like AI and telehealth and empowers patients towards digital health literacy. The initiative partners with the 
state's Health Information Exchange, Division of Information Technology, and other partners to improve digital access for rural providers and 
patients.

E.3. Short-term, 
limited-duration 
insurance 
(STLDI)
Rural populations consistently have higher uninsurance rates than their 
urban counterparts. Short -term, limited duration insurance (STLDI) plans 
may offer some rural individuals lower -cost, short-term coverage options. 
This State Policy Action captures whether a State restricts availability, 
use, or timeframe of STLDI beyond the latest federal guidance.
At baseline, NC did not 
restrict STLDI plans 
beyond the latest federal 
guidance
No State 
commitment (No 
STLDI plan 
restrictions 
beyond federal 
guidance)

North Dakota
State Snapshot: 2026 Release

North Dakota
Initiative Name Initiative Description
Strengthen and Stabilize Rural 
Health Workforce
Strengthens the rural healthcare workforce by creating new training pipelines and providing financial incentives for recruitment and 
retention. This initiative supports rural and tribal communities by offering programs like on-site childcare and hands-on training to 
attract and keep providers. It leverages technology and partnerships with higher education institutions to build a resilient workforce for 
the future.
Make North Dakota Healthy 
Again | Preventive Care, Healthy 
Eating 
Improves the health of North Dakotans by focusing on preventive care and healthy lifestyles to reduce chronic disease. This initiative 
promotes physical activity through the "North Dakota Moves Together" campaign and improves access to nutritious food through the
"Eat Well North Dakota" program. It builds on community wellness strategies by funding local grants and expanding behavioral health 
promotion programs like Parents Lead.
Bring High-Quality Health Care 
Closer to Home
Expands access to care by delivering services directly to rural and frontier communities through technology and mobile solutions. The 
initiative establishes telehealth access points in community locations and modernizes rural ambulance services to improve 
emergency response. It leverages a "Clinics Without Walls" strategy, using mobile clinics and remote monitoring to connect residents 
with primary and specialty care.
Connect T ech, Data and 
Providers for a Stronger North 
Dakota
Builds a unified rural health system by modernizing technology and connecting systems, data, and providers across the state. This 
initiative creates a secure, cloud-based platform to unify patient data, improving care coordination and clinical decision-making. It 
supports rural providers by establishing cooperative purchasing agreements for technology and uses artificial intelligence tohelp 
detect early signs of chronic disease.

State Policy 
Action Description Baseline Commitment Status
Associated 
Funding Received 
in Year 2 
C.3. Certificate 
of Need
Certificate of Need (CON) laws require healthcare providers to seek 
permission from the State before they can open new facilities or 
expand service offerings. Eliminating or loosening CON laws allows 
providers to establish new facilities in underserved rural regions 
without increased burden, addressing the scarcity of local care 
options. This State Policy Action captures State efforts to ease 
CON laws across several service and facility types.
At baseline, ND had limited 
CON regulations
No State 
commitment N/A $2.16M
D.2. Licensure 
Compacts
Licensure compacts provide clinicians with the opportunity to serve 
patients across State borders, increasing the supply of accessible 
rural health providers. This State Policy Action captures State 
participation in licensure compacts for physicians, nursing, EMS, 
psychology, and Physician Assistants.
At baseline, ND was a member of 
four of the five licensure 
compacts
ND committed to 
this State Policy 
Action
Complete: PA 
Compact $2.29M
D.3. Scope of 
Practice: PA, NP , 
Pharmacist, 
Dental Hygienist
Physician supply challenges in rural areas could be mitigated by 
expanding the scope of practice of other clinicians who have training 
and competency in caring for many of the cases currently limited to 
physician care. This State Policy Action captures State efforts to 
expand scope of practice for Physician Assistants, Nurse 
Practitioners, Pharmacists, and Dental Hygienists.
At baseline, ND had full scope of 
practice for PAs, NPs, and Dental 
Hygienists. Pharmacist scope of 
practice was considered 
restricted. 
ND committed to 
this State Policy 
Action
In Progress: 
Pharmacist SOP $2.96M

Rural populations consistently have higher uninsurance rates than 
their urban counterparts. Short -term, limited duration insurance 
(STLDI) plans may offer some rural individuals lower -cost, short-term 
coverage options. This State Policy Action captures whether a State 
restricts availability, use, or timeframe of STLDI beyond the latest 
federal guidance.

services and the presence of other policies to allow out -of-State 
clinicians to provide telehealth/remote care.

all three types of remote care 
services and had one type of 
policy in place to allow out-of-

telehealth/remote care

Ohio
State Snapshot: 2026 Release

Ohio
Initiative Name Initiative Description
Ohio Rural School Based 
Health Centers (SBHCs) 
Improves healthcare access for children and the community by establishing sustainable clinics on school (K-12 and college) campuses. 
These centers provide primary care, dental care, screenings, behavioral health services, and telehealth connections with specialty 
providers, reducing student absenteeism and creating a pipeline for future clinicians at the college level. The initiative operates through 
partnerships with Federally Qualified Health Centers (FQHCs), rural school districts, and the Ohio Department of Health (ODH).
OH Student Eye Exam (SEE), 
vision, hearing, and dental 
services for rural students
Expands mobile health services to provide vision, hearing, and dental care for students in rural areas. This program brings care directly to 
school campuses via mobile units, addressing the significant gap in follow-up care for students who fail initial screenings. It builds upon 
the existing OH SEE program by partnering with school districts and health providers, and universities across the state.
Ohio Rural Health 
Innovation Hubs
Establishes integrated networks of local providers to improve the quality, efficiency, and coordination of patient care in rural Ohio. These 
hubs support alliances among physicians, hospitals, and community resources to enhance patient outcomes while controlling costs. The 
initiative leverages partnerships with existing clinical networks and state agencies to help providers become self-sustaining.
Rural Ohio Emergency Care 
Transformation
Builds upon an existing pilot project to improve emergency response times and connect patients to the most appropriate level of care. 
This initiative scales alternate destination transport and treat-in-place options for rural communities across the state. It relies on 
partnerships between local EMS, hospitals, and other healthcare providers to reduce unnecessary emergency department visits.
Rural Health Workforce 
Pipeline and Development 
Projects
Develops a long-lasting pipeline of healthcare professionals by engaging students early and creating pathways to rural careers. The 
initiative coordinates activities between schools, colleges, and healthcare employers to provide work-based learning and career 
placement agreements. It supports these efforts by offering recruitment incentives and upskilling programs for community health workers 
and pharmacists.
$202.03M
Budget Period 1 
Funding
$201.93M
Budget Period 2 
Funding

Ohio
Initiative Name Initiative Description
Maternal and Infant 
Wellness Home Visiting in 
Rural Ohio 
Provides nurse-led, evidence-based home visiting services to mothers and infants in all rural and partially rural counties. Thisprogram 
supports families by providing education on topics like safe sleep, lactation, and early childhood mental health. It complements existing 
state funds and relies on partnerships with nurses, colleges of nursing, and home visiting organizations.
Rural Hospital Training and 
T echnical Assistance 
Center (RHTAC) 
Offers no-cost technical assistance, training, and guidance to help rural hospitals achieve financial solvency and sustainability. The 
center conducts workshops for healthcare leaders on best practices, such as joining an integrated network or developing formal 
sustainability plans. This initiative leverages the expertise of an experienced vendor to work directly with Ohio's critical access hospitals.
Electronic Medical Record 
Access for Pharmacies 
Equips pharmacists with the tools and training needed to operate under an expanded scope of practice and become an integratedpart of 
the patient care team. This initiative gives pharmacists access to electronic medical records to support point-of-care testing and prevent 
prescription drug misuse. It is implemented through a partnership between the Ohio Board of Pharmacy and the Ohio Pharmacists
Association.
Healthier Ohio Initiatives 
Engages rural communities in a collective movement to improve health outcomes by bridging medicine and preventive behaviors. The
program implements initiatives focused on nutrition, physical activity, and stress management in clinical settings, schools, and
community-serving agencies. It leverages partnerships with schools and community agencies to create a culture of wellness for the 
resident population.

B.2. Health and 
Lifestyle 
Rural populations are disproportionately impacted by chronic diseases. 
Promoting prevention through physical activity and proper nutrition can 
reduce overall cost of care burden and improve health outcomes. This State 
Policy Action captures whether a State requires schools to reestablish 
the Presidential Fitness Test.
At baseline, OH did not 
require schools to 
reestablish the 
Presidential Fitness Test

this State Policy 
Action
No Progress $1.14M
B.3. SNAP 
Waivers
SNAP provides food benefits to low -income families to supplement their 
grocery budget. Restricting the use of SNAP benefits on non -nutritious foods 
can help improve dietary intake and clinical indicators associated with long -
term disease in rural populations. This State Policy Action captures State 
efforts to obtain a USDA SNAP Food Restriction Waiver to prohibit 
purchase of non-nutritious items.
At baseline, OH had 
submitted a waiver, being 
processed by USDA
OH committed to 
this State Policy 
Action
Complete $6.64M
B.4. Nutrition 
Continuing 
Medical 
Education
Improved nutrition education among physicians through continuing medical 
education (CME) requirements can directly contribute to improving the 
health of Americans who live in rural areas given the disproportionate 
impact of chronic disease on rural America. This State Policy Action 
captures State efforts to require nutrition to be a component for CME.
At baseline, OH did not 
require nutrition to be 
included in CME for 
physicians
OH committed to 
this State Policy 
Action

Oklahoma
State Snapshot: 2026 Release

Oklahoma
Initiative Name Initiative Description
Innovating the Care Model
Expands access to primary and specialty care for rural Oklahomans by deploying digital solutions, transportation support, andexpansion 
of the non-physician workforce. This initiative establishes a statewide telestroke network, expands RPM for maternal health, and 
integrates behavioral health into primary care settings. It leverages partnerships with the Oklahoma Association of Regional Councils 
(OARC) and the Oklahoma State Department of Health (OSDH) and enhances community paramedicine, doulas, and school-based health 
services.
Moving Upstream
Empowers individuals and communities to build a healthier Oklahoma through innovative, consumer-focused prevention programs. This 
initiative supports community-led wellness hubs, expands the use of community health workers (CHWs) in hospitals, and provides 
funding for chronic disease management. It leverages partnerships with local health departments to connect residents with community-
based resources. Establishes a closed-loop community care platform, chronic disease management programs, consumer-facing 
technology for chronic disease prevention and management and behavioral health, lung cancer screening, and Presidential Fitness Test 
preparation funding.
Facilitating Regional 
Collaboration
Supports access to high-quality healthcare through strategic regionalization of services across the state. This initiative establishes a rural-
focused Clinically Integrated Network (CIN) to foster collaboration and creates a centralized EMS platform to improve efficiency. It builds 
upon existing relationships between rural hospitals and the to gain scale through shared services. Develops a Rural Regional 
Reorientation Plan to bring rural hospitals and ecosystem partners (outpatient providers, long-term care, payers) together to align on the 
future system of care grounded in the needs of rural communities and oriented toward improved access to upstream prevention and 
primary care.
Shifting to Value
Builds a path to value-based care for primary care, behavioral health, and hospital providers. This initiative provides capacity-building 
funds for primary care practices to support value-based clinical extension models and expands the Program of All-Inclusive Care for the 
Elderly (PACE). It supports providers serving dual-eligible populations by integrating Medicare and Medicaid financing to deliver 
comprehensive, team-based care.
$223.48M
Budget Period 1 
Funding
$227.10M
Budget Period 2 
Funding

Oklahoma
Initiative Name Initiative Description
Growing the Next 
Generation of Rural Talent
Creates a sustainable workforce pipeline by expanding training capacity and attracting providers to underserved areas. The initiative 
launches new rural residency programs for surgery, psychiatry, and OBGYN and provides relocation incentives for behavioral health 
providers. It leverages "Grow Your Own" programs, partnering with the Oklahoma Department of Career and T echnology Education to train 
high school students for healthcare careers.
Building Health Data Utility
Creates a connected rural health data ecosystem to support statewide planning and improve clinical decision-making. This initiative 
expands EHR access for smaller independent providers and enhances interoperability through the state health information exchange
(HIE). It is administered through the Oklahoma State Health Information Network Exchange (OKSHINE) to build an integrated analytics 
platform that enables real-time information sharing.

Oregon
State Snapshot: 2026 Release

Oregon
Initiative Name Initiative Description
Regional Partnerships & 
System Transformation
Applies regional solutions to enhance Oregon's shared healthcare infrastructure and promote financial stability in rural communities. This 
initiative funds local proposals to create and sustain partnerships between health care and social service entities. It leverages a hub-and-
spoke model to improve care coordination and build long-term sustainability, with a focus on at-risk services like maternity care.
Healthy Communities & 
Prevention
Bolsters rural health systems by expanding access to integrated primary care and social health services that promote prevention and care 
management. The initiative supports Oregonians across all life stages, from expanding nurse home-visiting programs for new families to 
community-based services that help seniors age in place. It leverages local partnerships and technology-driven solutions to address 
health needs across the entire lifespan.
Workforce Capacity & 
Resilience
Addresses the growing shortage of health care professionals in rural communities through targeted investments and innovative strategies. 
This initiative expands Oregon’s skilled health care workforce by creating new training opportunities and long-term capacity across the 
state. It supports the current workforce by enhancing provider wellness and retention through peer support and expanded tele-mentoring.
T echnology & Data 
Modernization
Transforms Oregon's rural health communities through investments in enhanced technological approaches and solutions includingAI and 
remote patient monitoring. This initiative ensures digital access and secure sharing of health information, reducing administrative burden 
and improving coordination across providers, patients, and systems. It focuses on support for providers and rural communitiesto
thoughtfully invest in technologies that meet their needs.
Tribal
Supports the Nine Federally Recognized Tribes of Oregon in implementing and enhancing projects that align with their individual priorities 
and goals. This initiative honors the self-determination and sovereignty of the Tribes, recognizing them as best positioned to lead 
healthcare transformation for their communities. It leverages partnerships between the Oregon Health Authority (OHA) and Tribal 
governments to ensure investments create long-lasting and sustainable impacts.

Pennsylvania
State Snapshot: 2026 Release

Pennsylvania
Initiative Name Initiative Description*
Maternal Health
Creates regional rural Maternal Health Hubs to serve as centralized access points for comprehensive, coordinated maternity care. The 
initiative links hospitals, community providers, and behavioral health partners to ensure every pregnant and postpartum person has access 
to a full spectrum of services. It builds on existing partnerships with Rural Care Collaboratives (RCCs) to address regional needs and 
expand access to care.
Behavioral Health
Expands crisis services and increases access to care through remote behavioral health consultation and a telehealth bridge clinic for 
same-day Substance Use Disorder (SUD) treatment. The program expands the rural behavioral health workforce by offering educational 
awards for peer and recovery specialists and developing a statewide training management system. This initiative leverages partnerships 
with the PA Office of Vocational Rehabilitation and county agencies to support rural communities.
Aging and Access 
Implements activities to reduce readmissions, build the nurse aide workforce, improve infection control, and simplify access to long-term 
care services statewide. The initiative funds targeted quality improvements in long-term care communities and expands the Program of All-
Inclusive Care for the Elderly (PACE) to underserved rural counties. It leverages the state's "Aging Our Way, PA" strategic plan to support 
older adults and rural providers.
EMS-Transportation
Modernizes emergency medical services and infrastructure to improve readiness and response capacity in rural communities. This initiative 
strengthens the EMS workforce through targeted recruitment, training, and mental health mentorship programs. It leverages partnerships 
with regional EMS councils and county Public Safety Answering Points (PSAPs) to ensure resources are dispatched efficiently.

$190.73M

Pennsylvania
Initiative Name Initiative Description*
T echnology and 
Infrastructure
Transforms the statewide health data infrastructure to improve patient care through innovative and connected digital technologies. This 
initiative strengthens Pennsylvania’s care network by expanding digital tools, mobile health units, and community telehealth hubs. It 
enables stronger connections between healthcare and community resources by leveraging partnerships with regional and statewide 
organizations.
Workforce
Strengthens and modernizes the rural health workforce by recruiting rural students into health careers and expanding clinicaltraining 
opportunities. This initiative builds a durable pipeline of providers by supporting innovative, team-based care models that makerural 
practice more attractive and sustainable. The program leverages partnerships with rural hospitals, community health centers, and
educational institutions to "Grow Our Own" workforce.
*Note: Pennsylvania’s RHTP design includes the establishment of 8 Rural Care Collaboratives (RCCs). Each RCC will identify local priorities and determine how funding is 
allocated, ensuring that investments reflect regional challenges and opportunities. Descriptions may vary among RCCs.

B.3. SNAP 
Waivers
The Supplemental Nutrition Assistance Program (SNAP) provides food 
benefits to low-income families to supplement their grocery budget. 
Restricting the use of SNAP benefits on non -nutritious foods can help 
improve dietary intake and clinical indicators associated with long -term 
disease in rural populations. This State Policy Action captures State 
efforts to obtain a USDA SNAP Food Restriction Waiver to prohibit 
purchase of non-nutritious items.
At baseline, PA had active 
legislation to create 
SNAP waiver
No State 
Commitment N/A $1.66M
C.3. Certificate 
of Need
Certificate of Need (CON) laws require healthcare providers to seek 
permission from the State before they can open new facilities or expand 
service offerings. Eliminating or loosening CON laws allows providers to 
establish new facilities in underserved rural regions without increased 
burden, addressing the scarcity of local care options. This State Policy 
Action captures State efforts to ease CON laws across several service 
and facility types.
At baseline, PA had no 
CON regulations across 
facility types 
No State 
commitment (No 
CON regulations 
in place)

E.3. Short-term, 
limited-duration 
insurance 
(STLDI)
Rural populations consistently have higher uninsurance rates than 
their urban counterparts. Short -term, limited duration insurance 
(STLDI) plans may offer some rural individuals lower -cost, short-term 
coverage options. This State Policy Action captures whether a 
State restricts availability, use, or timeframe of STLDI beyond the 
latest federal guidance.
At baseline, PA did not restrict 
STLDI plans beyond the latest 
federal guidance

restrictions 
beyond federal

Rhode Island
State Snapshot: 2026 Release

Rhode Island
Initiative Name Initiative Description
Integrated Rural Population 
Health Infrastructure
Establishes a comprehensive population health infrastructure by integrating clinical care hubs with community-led health networks. 
Transforms rural healthcare delivery by mobilizing local partners and resources to build healthier community environments. Delivers a 
strong clinical backbone that provides accessible, integrated medical care to rural residents.
Rural Community-
Integrated and Mobile 
Health Services
Utilizes rural Community Learning Centers (CLCs) as multipurpose hubs for uses such as dedicated telemedicine spaces equippedwith 
advanced audiovisual technology, digital stethoscopes, and electrocardiogram machines. Deploys trained personnel to actively assist 
residents in accessing and navigating virtual medical visits encompassing disability services, lifestyle interventions, and pre- and post-
natal support. Improves healthcare access for rural individuals by bridging the digital divide through hands-on technical support. 
Expanding Access to Rural 
Community Resources
Addresses critical physical barriers that prevent older adults and persons with disabilities from accessing medical offices and community 
spaces. Funds the installation of equipment and other accessibility improvements in public facilities, as well as assessmentsand 
trainings to support these investments. Complements broader healthcare infrastructure efforts by making existing community resources 
physically accessible to all residents.
Rural EMS Health Access 
and Integration
Transforms EMS into an integrated, patient-centered extension of the traditional healthcare system scaling Rhode Island’s Mobile
Integrated Health–Community Paramedicine (MIH-CP). Delivers preventive, urgent, and follow-up care directly within rural communities 
to improve patient outcomes. Reduces heavy reliance on emergency departments by resolving minor medical issues on-site.
Rural Hospital-at-Home 
Program
Strengthens local access to acute and post-acute services by establishing a scalable, home-based hospital care model. Delivers safe, 
high-quality medical treatment in patients' homes as a direct substitute for traditional inpatient admission. Replicates proven clinical 
standards to ensure safe and effective acute-level care outside of hospital walls while reducing costs.
$156.17M
Budget Period 1 
Funding
$158.17M
Budget Period 2 
Funding

Rhode Island
Initiative Name Initiative Description
Expanding Behavioral 
Health Services Availability 
in Rural Regions
Expands mental health, substance use disorder , and crisis intervention services across rural regions with the creation of a 24/7 behavioral 
health and crisis stabilization center to ensure timely support for adults 18 and above. Improves patient engagement in treatment 
programs through the introduction of recovery community centers and builds stronger linkages to long-term recovery networks. 
Strengthens statewide recovery capacity to provide sustainable, community-based behavioral health resources.
Strengthening Rural Oral 
Health Delivery Through 
Innovation and Integration
Improves oral healthcare delivery and reduces unnecessary emergency department visits for dental complaints in underserved areas. 
Enhances provider capacity across the state through specialized clinical training and remote professional consultation. Creates a 
sustainable network of dental care capable of treating individuals with complex needs close to home.
Building Capacity for Block 
Island Health and Human 
Services
Integrates clinical hub concepts, enhanced emergency services, health technology infrastructure, and workforce development tosupport 
Rhode Island’s only rural health clinic in Block Island. Expands the existing community medicine program to deliver home-based, 
preventive, and follow-up care and aims to establish the first Program of All-Inclusive Care for the Elderly (PACE). Targets elderly and 
chronically ill residents to improve their overall quality of life.
Modernizing Health Care 
Delivery for the 
Narragansett Indian Tribe
Partners with the Tribe to modernize local health infrastructure, strengthen workforce capacity, and enhance care coordination. Deploys 
digital diagnostic tools, remote patient monitoring, and integrated electronic health records to improve data accuracy. Accelerates early 
disease detection and chronic disease management through advanced technology.
Rural Workforce Program
Builds a stable and highly skilled rural health workforce by expanding clinical training opportunities for medical and dentalproviders. 
Strengthens local education-to-employment pathways while supporting professional career advancement. Provides targeted recruitment 
and retention incentives,to establish a solid workforce foundation for other health initiatives, and tuition support to increase the supply of 
qualified rural-serving healthcare professionals.
