Rhode Island
Initiative Name Initiative Description
Supporting Hospitals and 
Primary Care in Value-
Based Payment Transition
Supports the long-term financial sustainability of rural hospitals and primary care practices through technical assistance, infrastructure 
development, and readiness for value-based payment arrangements. These investments will strengthen provider capacity to participate in 
innovative payment models, including Rhode Island’s Achieving Healthcare Efficiency through Accountable Design (AHEAD) Model,while 
advancing care coordination, quality improvement, and sustainable rural healthcare delivery.
Rural Health Information 
T echnology Modernization 
Program
Equips rural healthcare providers with a Rhode Island wide interoperable electronic health record system to reduce administrative 
burdens. Streamlines secure data exchange to facilitate better care coordination and continuity across different systems. Distributes 
infrastructure grants to help providers acquire digital tools for telehealth and workflow optimization.
Rural Health Data and 
Workforce Tracking System
Develops integrated data systems to track upstream social drivers of health and coordinate patient care. Connects community-based 
organizations with clinical providers to streamline communication. Informs future public investments in targeted preventive and non-
clinical health interventions and develops comprehensive provider tracking system to assess workforce distribution.

South Carolina
State Snapshot: 2026 Release

South Carolina
Initiative Name Initiative Description
Connections to Care
Expands digital infrastructure by implementing electronic health records, remote patient monitoring, telehealth services and a statewide 
resource database platform to improve care coordination and access. Assists health care entities in transitions from a paper-based 
system to a fully electronic medical record system with integrated modules, to improve interoperability, reduce duplication, and support 
value-based care, as well as to improve care coordination and streamline admissions. Implements a patient-facing digital intake platform 
in rural clinics to streamline documentation and identify drivers of health outcomes at the point of care. Supports purchasing standards-
based (to promote interoperability) remote patient monitoring (RPM) equipment for diabetes, hypertension, chronic obstructive
pulmonary disease (COPD), and heart disease patients, and the monitoring infrastructure for providers, with staff support formonitoring 
and troubleshooting.
Leveling Up
Scales successful pilot programs statewide, focusing on chronic disease management, pediatric care quality, and workforce 
development. This initiative expands proven local and regional programs, such as care coordination models and school mental health 
programs, to reach all rural communities. The program enhances Graduate Medical Education (GME) by partnering with rural hospitals to 
improve training experiences, scales proven chronic disease programs and successful pilot programs, and conducts comprehensive 
needs assessments to identify gaps in access, infrastructure, and services, ensuring future investments are data-driven and sustainable.
Wellness Within Reach
Deploys mobile health units, crisis response teams, and pop-up clinics to bring care directly to underserved populations. Supports 
mobile crisis response improvements, expands community care sites including permanent clinics, seasonal pop-ups, mobile vans, and 
intermittent service locations, emergency medical services. Establishes community-based health kiosks with equipment and supplies for 
a 24-hour pharmacy kiosk that distributes necessary medications in rural healthcare locations.

Budget Period 1 
Funding
$195.99M

South Carolina
Initiative Name Initiative Description
Shoring Up Sustainability
Strengthens rural healthcare systems through targeted investments in workforce recruitment, facility upgrades, and provider training. This 
initiative establishes healthcare workforce grants which include providing one-time signing bonuses to recruit quality providerswith a five-
year commitment. Enhances rural health facilities and establishes a masterclass training series to support partnerships with large 
hospital systems and rural hospitals for assistance in developing a strategic plan. Expands partnerships between rural familymedicine 
physicians and obstetricians/gynecologists (OB-GYNs) to create a prenatal care network and forms a provider collaborative to assist new 
entities with planning and implementation.
T ech Catalyst Fund
Supports rural health technology startups and community-based innovations to drive long-term health and economic improvements. This 
initiative accelerates innovation by investing in local entrepreneurs and scalable technologies that improve chronic disease management 
across South Carolina. 
South Carolina is also implementing a Rural Technology Catalyst Fund, which promotes consumer-facing, technology-driven solutions for chronic 
disease prevention and management in rural populations.

South Dakota
State Snapshot: 2026 Release

South Dakota
Initiative Name Initiative Description
T ech and Data Connection 
for a Healthier South Dakota
Modernizes healthcare delivery through a comprehensive approach to digital health infrastructure and information exchange. This initiative 
provides funding to help healthcare facilities adopt electronic health records (EHRs) and unifies siloed data into a single platform. It 
leverages the South Dakota Health Data Atlas to enable providers and policymakers to make better data-driven decisions.
Building a Sustainable Rural 
Healthcare Workforce 
Strengthens the state's rural health workforce by offering tiered sign-on bonuses and relocation assistance to attract new professionals. 
This initiative supports the existing workforce with funding for education and certification to improve retention in high-need rural areas. The 
program relies on partnerships with technical colleges and universities to create a stable and skilled workforce.
Expand and Strengthen 
Rural Community Health 
Worker (CHW) Workforce 
Establishes a robust community health worker (CHW) and community health representative (CHR) workforce to serve as a bridge between 
clinical care and community services. The initiative develops a CHW/CHR training and certification program and provides financial support 
to rural healthcare providers to hire and deploy CHWs. It partners with the South Dakota Department of Health and local health systems to 
improve care coordination for underserved populations.
Rural Health Forward: 
Training and Resource Hub
Creates a centralized hub to deliver best-in-class training, technical assistance, and leadership development for rural healthcare providers 
and allied health workers. The initiative helps rural facilities adapt to new care delivery models and improve financial stability. This program 
leverages partnerships with state universities, medical schools, and long-term care and home health agencies to ensure providershave the 
tools to succeed.
Medicaid Primary 
Accountable Care 
Transformation 
Improves health outcomes and lowers costs by moving primary care practices from a fee-for-service model to a value-based payment
system. This initiative offers flexible, capitated payments that give providers the resources and autonomy to deliver patient-centered care. 
The program will leverage South Dakota Medicaid's managed care program to incentivize quality outcomes over volume.
$189.48M
Budget Period 1 
Funding
$187.65M
Budget Period 2 
Funding

South Dakota
Initiative Name Initiative Description
Medicaid Rural Health 
Access and Quality Grants 
Preserves access to critical services by providing direct financial support to rural hospitals and clinics to improve qualityand modernize 
their facilities. The grants will support professional services such as financial, legal, and organizational change management, and regional 
assessments and partnerships that strengthen coordination and sustainability. The grants will be awarded through competitive Requests 
for Proposals (RFPs).
Strengthening Chronic 
Disease Management 
Reduces the impact of chronic conditions by providing targeted rural funding and expanding provider training. The initiative empowers 
patients and their families by offering essential caregiver support and chronic disease self-management programs. It leverages 
partnerships with local providers and public health agencies to improve long-term health outcomes for rural residents.
Regional Maternal and 
Infant Health Hubs 
Improves health outcomes for mothers and infants by creating regional hubs that provide comprehensive, coordinated care. Thisinitiative 
deploys multidisciplinary teams, including obstetricians, behavioral health specialists, community health workers, and tribaldoulas, to 
support women from pregnancy through the postpartum period. The program is modeled after successful maternal health programs and
leverages partnerships with local hospitals and clinics.
Integrated Behavioral Health 
through CCBHC & 
Collaborative Care 
Transforms behavioral health in rural South Dakota by implementing the Certified Community Behavioral Health Clinic (CCBHC) model 
statewide. This initiative ensures same-day access to care and 24/7 crisis response for individuals with mental health and substance use 
needs. It also expands the Collaborative Care Model (CoCM) to integrate behavioral health services into primary care settings.
Enhancing Sustainable 
Emergency Medical 
Services 
Creates a coordinated, regional system to ensure timely and sustainable emergency medical services across the state. This initiative 
establishes regional EMS hubs to improve response times and provides funding to upgrade equipment and training for local agencies. It 
leverages telemedicine and advanced technology to improve patient outcomes and enhance real-time data collection and sharing.

Tennessee
Initiative Name Initiative Description
Healthcare Resiliency 
Program: Service Line 
Expansion & Co-Location
Utilizes the proven Healthcare Resiliency Program (HRP) model to improve health outcomes. Expands access to comprehensive healthcare 
for rural communities through a competitive grant process. This initiative helps rural hospitals and clinics add or expand needed services 
like perinatal care, behavioral health, and primary care. 
Last Mile T eams
Unifies multiple efforts to expand emergency care and bring integrated health services to hard-to-reach areas. The program fundsnew 
ambulances, expands Emergency Medical Technician (EMT) training, and deploys mobile health clinics. It builds upon existing programs 
like Project Rural Recovery and the Community Health Access and Navigation in T ennessee (CHANT) program to connect pregnant and 
post-partum women with critical care.
Optimizing Rural Health 
Care
Increases access to primary, public, and dental health services for uninsured adults in Tennessee. It expands the Uninsured Adult 
Healthcare Safety Net Program to counties currently lacking providers. The initiative uses the state's network of local health departments to 
connect patients with services like specialty care, medication assistance, and transportation.
Memory Care Assessment 
Network
Improves access to diagnosis and treatment for rural adults experiencing Alzheimer’s Disease and Related Dementias (ADRD) through a 
hub-and-spoke model. Modeled after successful programs in other states, it connects specialized Memory Assessment Clinics with 
Neuropsychiatry resources. The network uses telehealth and trained Dementia Navigators to overcome geographic and workforce barriers.
Rural Capacity Building
Develop rural providers' capacity by establishing leadership pathways and expanding the behavioral and community health workforce. This 
initiative leverages Tennessee's successful Community Health Worker program through partnerships with academic centers and T ennCare 
to improve health outcomes for rural residents.
$206.89M
Budget Period 1 
Funding
$210.34M
Budget Period 2 
Funding

Tennessee
Initiative Name Initiative Description
Dental Pilot
Enhances investments in the rural dental workforce to improve oral health access across the state. Building on a successful pilot, this 
initiative recruits dental providers and establishes new dental access points in underserved communities. It focuses on integrating 
preventive oral health services into Dental Health Professional Shortage Areas (HPSAs).
Healthcare Resiliency 
Program: Maternal and Child 
Health
Builds a safer start for every mother and child in rural Tennessee by strengthening systems of care. The Healthcare Resiliency Program 
(HRP) will fund projects to expand maternity care capacity, improve perinatal education, and enhance data systems. It is guided by the 
state's Maternal and Infant Health Strategic Plans.
Value-Based Payment: 
Maternal, Hospitals, Dental
Catalyzes the shift to value-based payment (VBP) models for rural hospitals, obstetric providers, and dental clinics. This initiative 
strengthens infrastructure and workforce readiness to support participation in programs that reward high-quality care. It focuses on 
coordinated models like the Patient-Centered Obstetric Medical Home, the Rural Hospital VBP Capacity Program, and the Patient-Centered 
Dental Home.
Healthcare Resiliency 
Program: Make Rural TN 
Healthy Again (MaRTHA)
Expands competitive grants for community-driven prevention efforts across Tennessee. This initiative allows providers and community 
organizations to deliver integrated services that address the root causes of poor health. Projects may include establishing community 
health centers, expanding telehealth, or hiring school nurses.
Rural Health Improvement 
Grants
Funds competitive grants for locally-led prevention, nutrition, and active living projects. It uses County Health Councils to deploy grants 
that fund health improvement projects and build community capacity. The initiative expands access to health-promoting environments like 
parks, trails, and farmers markets.

Funding
$210.34M

Tennessee
Initiative Name Initiative Description
Healthcare Resiliency 
Program: Rural Non-
Emergency Transportation
Develops and scales solutions to reduce transportation barriers for people in rural Tennessee. The Tennessee Rural Non-Emergency
Transportation (RNET) Initiative will create an integrated coordination system for accessing primary, preventive, and specialty care. It builds 
on successful hospital-led shuttle programs and community-based ride partnerships.
Healthcare Resiliency 
Program (HRP): Health-T ech
Provides competitive grants for rural health technology infrastructure to empower providers. This initiative helps rural hospitals, clinics, and 
health systems implement scalable and secure digital solutions. It builds on the success of the HRP to improve access and clinical 
integration.
Statewide Health 
Information Exchange
Develops a statewide Health Information Exchange (HIE) to connect rural providers, hospitals, and payers. This initiative will create a 
secure, interoperable data infrastructure to enable seamless data sharing. A public-private partnership will leverage existing cloud 
technologies to build and launch the HIE.
TN Community Compass 
Expands Tennessee's successful closed-loop referral system to connect individuals with community-based organizations. TN Community 
Compass (TNCC) addresses health-related social needs like food, housing, and transportation. This expansion will scale integration across 
key state agencies to build a single, interoperable referral platform.
Rural Health Innovation 
Catalyst
Connects innovation offices to rural providers to advance health technology. Led by the University of T ennessee Health Science Center's 
Rural Center of Excellence (CoE), this initiative accelerates rural health transformation. It provides technical assistance and grants to help 
rural providers adopt technology-enabled solutions.

Budget Period 1 
Funding
$210.34M
Budget Period 2

Tennessee
Initiative Name Initiative Description
Statewide eConsult 
Platform
Establishes a statewide eConsult platform to improve specialty care access for uninsured individuals in rural T ennessee. Thissecure digital 
system allows primary care providers to consult with specialists electronically, reducing the need for in-person visits. The platform 
addresses the critical shortage of specialty care in rural areas.
Comprehensive Health 
Workforce Pipeline
Builds a comprehensive rural health workforce pipeline to create a sustainable supply of clinicians and administrators. This initiative 
expands and aligns the full continuum of workforce development, from early exposure to advanced practice. Investments includeresidency 
programs, recruitment incentives, and early exposure programs in schools.

Waivers

grocery budget. Restricting the use of SNAP benefits on non -nutritious foods 
can help improve dietary intake and clinical indicators associated with long -
term disease in rural populations. This State Policy Action captures State 
efforts to obtain a USDA SNAP Food Restriction Waiver to prohibit 
purchase of non-nutritious items.

submitted a waiver, being 
processed by USDA

C.3. Certificate 
of Need
Certificate of Need (CON) laws require healthcare providers to seek 
permission from the State before they can open new facilities or expand 
service offerings. Eliminating or loosening CON laws allows providers to 
establish new facilities in underserved rural regions without increased 
burden, addressing the scarcity of local care options. This State Policy 
Action captures State efforts to ease CON laws across several 
service and facility types.
At baseline, TN had moderate 
CON regulations

Texas
State Snapshot: 2026 Release

Texas
Initiative Name Initiative Description
Make Rural T exans Healthy 
Again
Part 1 issues targeted direct awards to Rural Hospital Districts and authorities with a publicly owned and operated hospital within their 
jurisdiction. Part 2 issued target grants through a competitive process to public and private rural hospitals to enhance community-based 
prevention, wellness, and nutrition programs. Targets the reduction of critical chronic conditions including diabetes, cardiovascular 
disease, chronic respiratory illness, and obesity among rural residents. Promotes healthier lifestyles through localized education and 
wellness services to improve long-term medical outcomes.
Rural Texas Patients in the 
Driver's Seat
Invests in technology to establish consumer-facing health portals that engage patients and facilitate the exchange of health information 
between patients, providers, and payers. Empowers rural residents to communicate directly with their healthcare teams throughsecure 
messaging and virtual visits. Provides providers and payers with access to patient medical documentation and health information to make 
informed care and coverage decisions.
Lone Star Advanced AI and 
T elehealth
Invest in AI and telehealth technologies to assist rural health care providers in their work to predict and improve patience outcomes, 
maintain care and more efficiently adjust medication and therapy for patience and linking rural primary care providers and their patients 
directly to specialized clinical experts. Funding also includes establishing local, patient-focused hubs where they can receive telehealth 
care.
The Next Generation of the 
Small T own Doctor and 
T eam
Strengthens the rural healthcare workforce by surrounding local physicians with midlevel practitioners and allied health professionals. 
Delivers comprehensive, community-based medical care to rural residents by expanding local clinical teams. Reduces regional shortages 
of essential health professionals by implementing targeted recruitment for specific provider types and retention for providers. Locally driven 
efforts will focus on developing career paths for local high school students, providing educational awards for recent high school graduates, 
offering relocation or signing bonuses for early, mid, or late career professionals, creating a new residency training program, fellowship or 
combination program, including partnering with academic institutions or an existing teaching hospital.

Budget Period 1 
Funding
$281.98M

Texas
Initiative Name Initiative Description
Unified Care Infrastructure 
and Rural Cyber Protection
Establishes a unified care infrastructure and bolsters cybersecurity defenses across rural healthcare providers. Deploys advanced 
managed security solutions including endpoint detection and response, comprehensive, all-time security operations center monitoring, 
and comprehensive user training to protect sensitive patient records and maintain system integrity. Aligns local technology standards to 
safeguard rural health networks against digital threats.
Infrastructure and Capital 
Improvement for Rural Texas
Modernizes rural healthcare services by allowing rural local clinics, hospitals, pharmacies, EMS, and behavioral health providers to add and 
replace outdated equipment. Upgrades essential technology within public health offices and emergency medical systems to improve 
overall patient care. Adheres to required capital limitations to strategically enhance existing facilities without costly newconstruction.

Utah
State Snapshot: 2026 Release

Utah
Initiative Name Initiative Description
Making Rural Utahns 
Healthy: Preventive Action 
and Transformation for 
Health (PATH)
Advances rural health through innovations in nutrition, physical activity, and built environments to establish lifelong healthy behaviors and 
impact maternal and behavioral health. Establishes community care hubs using regional stakeholder groups to create integratedmodels 
that improve physical and mental health and funding local partners to support efforts including nutritious foods and physicalactivity. 
Targets rural Utahns across their entire lifespan to prevent chronic disease and support behavioral health.
Rural Incentive and Skill 
Expansion (RISE)
Invests in career pathways starting in grade school to build a sustainable rural healthcare workforce. Connects local education and 
expanded clinical training directly to regional healthcare needs and expands on the capacity of rural clinical preceptor programs across 
various provider types. Aligns with state leadership goals to increase the total number of primary care providers in rural counties by five 
percent.
Sustaining Health 
Infrastructure for 
Transformation (SHIFT)
Transforms preventive care through targeted enhancements to local service delivery including the introduction of a 24/7 crisis stabilization 
center and multi-purpose facilities delivering preventive and specialist care services. Builds a resilient, interconnected system centered on 
proactive wellness, early clinical intervention, and care delivered at home. Addresses interconnected economic, infrastructure, and 
workforce barriers that cause disproportionately poor health outcomes in rural populations.
Financial Approaches for 
Sustainable Transformation 
(FAST)
Resolves core financial and care delivery challenges that hinder the viability of rural healthcare in Utah. Transitions localproviders to value-
based payment models and proactive care while streamlining revenue cycles for efficiency. Pilots alternative funding models to secure 
reliable specialist access for rural communities and allow patients to receive care from home.
Leveraging Innovation for 
Facilitated T elehealth (LIFT)
Improves physical and behavioral healthcare access in rural communities through scalable, data-informed telehealth strategies. Delivers 
hands-on training and technical assistance to facilitate effective technology implementation and lasting impact. Expands timely access to 
high-quality care to address lower cancer survival rates and higher rates of suicide.
$195.74M
Budget Period 1 
Funding
$191.92M
Budget Period 2 
Funding

Utah
Initiative Name Initiative Description
Shared Utilities for 
Partnered Provider 
Operational Resources and 
T echnology (SUPPORT)
Partners with rural facilities to build common critical digital, technological, and administrative infrastructure across facilities. Leverages 
economies of scale by offering collaborative opportunities and shared operational resources. Preserves local operational autonomy while 
helping rural clinics and hospitals reduce infrastructure costs, and providers limit time spent on administrative and non-clinical tasks.
Leveraging Interoperability 
Networks to Connect 
Services (LINCS)
Creates a connected health ecosystem by modernizing the secure electronic exchange of health data among rural clinics, hospitals, and 
public health entities. Aligns with the Centers for Medicare and Medicaid’s Interoperability Framework to advance open, standards-based 
data exchange. Positions the state at the forefront of digital healthcare coordination through secure, real-time data sharing and ongoing 
recruitment and training for associated staff.

waiver approved by USDA

er in place)

C.3. Certificate 
of Need
Certificate of Need (CON) laws require healthcare providers to seek 
permission from the State before they can open new facilities or expand 
service offerings. Eliminating or loosening CON laws allows providers to 
establish new facilities in underserved rural regions without increased 
burden, addressing the scarcity of local care options. This State Policy 
Action captures State efforts to ease CON laws across several service 
and facility types.
At baseline, UT had no 
CON regulations across 
facility types 
No State 
commitment (No 
CON regulations in 
place)

Vermont
State Snapshot: 2026 Release

Vermont
Initiative Name Initiative Description
Regionalization and 
Innovative Care Strategies
Conducts detailed strategic planning and implementation to establish a transformed, regional system of care ensuring health services are 
right sized and optimally distributed. Expands immediate access to community-based care through new urgent care and mobile medical 
units. Invests in specialized equipment like dialysis units and ventilators to deliver high-acuity services to nursing home residents.
Establishing a Clinically 
Integrated Network of 
Shared Services
Fosters collaboration and partnerships across the continuum of healthcare providers in the state collaborating with an organizing entity to 
implement initiative components. Implements a system of shared services to produce operational efficiencies, lower costs, andpreserve 
patient choice. Funds the purchase of necessary clinical technologies, including telehealth, electronic consultations, remotepatient 
monitoring, and artificial intelligence scribes.
Strengthening Primary Care
Creates a modern, enhanced iteration of the state's existing Blueprint model to upgrade the current model originating in 2005. Focuses on 
integrated treatment for complex chronic conditions, evidence-based workforce training, and analytical and technical practice 
transformation assistance. Delivers continuous quality improvements to establish a highly coordinated learning and performance 
monitoring network.
Health Care Workforce 
Development
Strengthens the state's rural healthcare pipeline through strategic investments in recruitment, training, and retention. Enables local clinical 
providers to operate at the top of their professional licenses. Fosters a sustainable network of skilled professionals to improve overall care 
delivery in underserved areas and encourages partnerships among hospitals, educational institutions, and community partners.
Price Transparency and 
Insurance Competition
Increases access to affordable, high-quality care for rural residents by improving pricing transparency and insurance market participation. 
Enhances the state's ability to analyze cost, quality, and access metrics through a new technical platform accessible to policymakers, 
regulators, and the public. Supports a statewide assessment to evaluate options for expanding affordable health coverage options.

Virginia
State Snapshot: 2026 Release

Virginia
Initiative Name Initiative Description
CareIQ
Advances innovative technologies that promote efficient care delivery, improved provider satisfaction, and enhanced patient outcomes in 
rural areas. Enables local healthcare providers to leapfrog outdated service models to deliver highly effective, data-informed medical 
treatments. Supports clinical decision-making through the deployment of modern digital health tools. Supports early-stage health
technology start-ups based in Virginia, providers in adoption new, pre-selected tools to improve productivity through better decision 
support, documentation automation, workflow efficiency, and adopting upgraded EHR, cybersecurity and data analytics platforms, and 
hospitals to support scaling of remote patient monitoring (RPM). 
Homegrown Health Heroes
Grows the rural provider workforce while providing clear career pipelines and economic opportunities for rural Virginians. Builds a 
sustainable local talent pipeline to address critical medical staffing shortages in underserved communities. Encourages residents to train 
and remain in their home counties as highly skilled clinical professionals. Funds residency slots across rural health systemsin priority 
specialties, supports stand up of mobile labs, and expands capacity for existing and new healthcare credentials. 
Connected Care, Closer to 
Home
Rewires how rural healthcare is delivered by bringing essential services directly into remote communities. Expands local access to 
primary, urgent, specialty, substance use disorder, dental, vision, and maternity care. Supports mobile care units, telehealth kiosks, 
“clinics in a box” , e consults, and development of “hubs” . Supports local EMS in training, staffing, and equipping to expand scope of 
services. Supports innovative maternal care through technical assistance for RPM. 
Live Well, Together
Focuses on preventing chronic disease through a series of community-based, whole-health sub-initiatives. Supports Food as Medicine 
sub-initiative by piloting food pharmacies that offer produce, nutrition counseling, and medically tailored meals for food insecure patients 
with or at risk of chronic disease or recently discharged from the hospital. Pilots integrated chronic disease management programs to 
scale approaches to monitor and support high needs patients. Supports minor renovation and repurposing of community spaces into 
recreational spaces for structured physical activity programs, sports, and wellness education. Provides community-based education and 
engagement to support dual-eligible seniors in navigation of health care coverage and enrollment in integrated care plans. 
Virginia is also implementing a Rural Technology Catalyst Fund, which promotes consumer-facing, technology-driven solutions for chronic disease 
prevention and management in rural populations.

Washington
State Snapshot: 2026 Release

Washington
Initiative Name Initiative Description
Ignite innovation in 
Washington’s rural 
hospitals
Strengthens the financial health and sustainability of The Rural Collaborative (TRC), 30 independent rural hospitals, throughnew business 
models, technology, and forms of cooperation. This initiative expands a rural health network to create greater efficiencies and broadens 
the pathway for hospitals to participate in value-based care arrangements, shared staffing, and group purchasing. It leverages 
partnerships with the TRC and the Washington State Hospital Association (WSHA) to improve hospital operating efficiency.
Prevent disease and 
manage care in community 
settings
Builds and expands programs that support rural residents in settings outside the walls of clinical systems. The initiative expands the 
community-based workforce, develops dementia-capable communities, and modernizes emergency medical services transport to 
reduce delays for time-sensitive emergencies. It leverages partnerships with existing community hubs like senior centers, churches, and 
schools to promote prevention and connect people to social services.
Invest in the health of 
Native families 
Improves the health and well-being of Native families by making critical investments in workforce development and care coordination. 
This initiative supports the training and hiring of Community Health Representatives, doulas , and other community-based workersto help 
families navigate complex health systems. It fosters strategic partnerships between Tribes and local hospitals through written care 
coordination agreements.
Adopt technology and data 
solutions  to enable health 
improvements
Provides start-up funding and technical assistance for new and emerging technologies that increase efficiency, access, and quality in 
Washington's rural health care system. This initiative expands remote care services and provider mentorship through Project ECHO
(Extension for Community Healthcare Outcomes) and invests in a provider technology fund for tools like AI, remote patient monitoring, 
and telehealth. It leverages partnerships with the University of Washington and other medical centers to bring expertise to rural 
communities.

$177.06M

Funding

Washington
Initiative Name Initiative Description
Develop Washington’s rural 
workforce to support rural 
communities
Launches a comprehensive strategy to strengthen the rural health workforce by focusing on key roles like nurses, primary careproviders, 
and dental hygienists. This initiative expands rural family residency programs and supports "grow-your-own" training models thatcreate 
pathways from high school to health careers. It leverages the Washington, Wyoming, Alaska, Montana, and Idaho regional collaborative 
(WWAMI) and Washington State University (WSU) to develop well-trained physicians prepared for rural practice.
Expand and sustain 
Washington’s rural 
behavioral health system
Addresses the urgent and unmet behavioral health needs in rural Washington by expanding mobile crisis supports and investing in the 
workforce. This initiative helps transition behavioral health clinics to the Certified Community Behavioral Health Clinics (CCBHCs)
model and improves access to school-based services for youth. It leverages the National Behavioral Health Crisis Care Guidance 
framework developed by the Substance Abuse and Mental Health Services Administration (SAMHSA) and investing in local EMS to provide 
a full spectrum of care.

West Virginia
State Snapshot: 2026 Release

West Virginia
Initiative Name Initiative Description
Connected Care Grid
Builds the infrastructure to bring virtual and in-person primary, preventive, urgent, and specialist care access to people on-demand. This 
initiative bridges the gap between urban and rural communities by creating an integrated network that connects clinics, homes, and 
hospitals through modern technology. It expands specialty care access by leveraging partnerships with the WV Health Information Network 
(WVHIN) and local provider hubs.
Rural Health Link
Addresses transportation barriers by enabling access to a unified health-mobility platform for rural residents. The initiative connects 
citizens to transport options like public buses, rideshares, and volunteers for non-emergency medical needs. It builds on existing Medicaid 
non-emergency medical transportation (NEMT) and regional transit programs by partnering with the Departments of Health and 
Transportation to reduce missed appointments.
Mountain State Care Force
Addresses persistent health workforce shortages by developing home-grown talent and attracting providers to the state. This initiative 
expands rural residency programs and offers incentives for providers who commit to practicing in rural areas. It builds on existing state 
workforce programs by partnering with West Virginia's medical schools, the community and technical college system, and K-12 education.
Smart Care Catalyst
Helps rural providers reduce administrative burden and modernize operations by focusing on value and quality. This initiativesupports the 
transition from fee-for-service to outcomes-driven payment models that improve both the quality and affordability of healthcare.It will 
partner with managed care organizations and commercial payers to develop standardized, multi-payer value-based care models.
Health to Prosperity
Tackles health issues that pose barriers to employment by connecting adults with chronic conditions to coordinated care and job 
placement. The initiative helps employees stay healthy by embedding wellness and preventive services directly in the workplace. It expands 
on successful state programs by partnering with employers and Medicaid managed care organizations (MCOs).

West Virginia
Initiative Name Initiative Description
Personal Health Accelerator
Empowers West Virginians to take ownership of their health through nutritious food, exercise, and local care connections. Theinitiative 
supports nutrition and lifestyle improvements by integrating wellness promotion and tracking into medical data systems. It creates synergies 
with the Connected Care Grid by engaging local, private sector, and provider partners to build sustainable programs.
HealthT ech Appalachia
Incubates and scales breakthrough technologies that improve rural health and productivity while drawing private investment toexpand 
statewide impact. This initiative fosters tech-enabled innovations to address chronic disease management and Substance Use Disorder 
recovery. The program will partner with universities, entrepreneurs, and investors to test and scale technologies that make care accessible 
in rural settings.
West Virginia is also implementing a Rural Technology Catalyst Fund, which promotes consumer-facing, technology-driven solutions for chronic disease 
prevention and management in rural populations.

D.2. Licensure 
Compacts
Licensure compacts provide clinicians with the opportunity to serve 
patients across State borders, increasing the supply of accessible rural 
health providers. This State Policy Action captures State participation 
in licensure compacts for physicians, nursing, EMS, psychology, and 
Physician Assistants.
At baseline, WV was a 
member of the five 
licensure compacts

commitment 
(Member of all five 
licensure 
compacts)

D.3. Scope of 
Practice
Physician supply challenges in rural areas could be mitigated by 
expanding the scope of practice of other clinicians who have training and 
competency in caring for many of the cases currently limited to physician 
care. This State Policy Action captures State efforts to expand scope of 
practice for Physician Assistants, Nurse Practitioners, Pharmacists, 
and Dental Hygienists.
At baseline, WV had 
advanced scope of practice 
(SOP) for PAs, reduced SOP 
for NPs, limited authority for 
Pharmacists, and semi-
restricted SOP for Dental 
Hygienists

E.3. Short-term, 
limited-duration 
insurance 
(STLDI)
Rural populations consistently have higher uninsurance rates than their 
urban counterparts. Short -term, limited duration insurance (STLDI) plans 
may offer some rural individuals lower -cost, short-term coverage options. 
This State Policy Action captures whether a State restricts availability, 
use, or timeframe of STLDI beyond the latest federal guidance.
At baseline, WV did not 
restrict STLDI plans beyond 
the latest federal guidance

commitment (No

restrictions 
beyond federal 
guidance)

F .1. Remote 
Care Services
Remote care services can help expand access to care by allowing 
clinicians to provide care to rural residents from another location via 
telehealth, remote patient monitoring, or other modalities. This State 
Policy Action captures State Medicaid coverage of remote care 
services and the presence of other policies to allow out -of-State 
clinicians to provide telehealth/remote care.
At baseline, WV Medicaid 
covered two types of remote 
care services and had 
policies in place to allow 
out-of-State clinicians to 
provide telehealth/remote 
care

Wisconsin
State Snapshot: 2026 Release

Wisconsin
Initiative Name Initiative Description
Rural Talent Recruitment 
and Retention
Combats severe provider shortages in rural areas by training new healthcare workers and recruiting them to locations of need.Focuses 
investments on building long-term innovative workforce systems and supporting career pathways for rural health care providers. Boosts 
adoption of community health workers (CHWs) and developing Medicaid reimbursement pathway for CHW services. Establishes a 
sustainable support infrastructure to retain clinical professionals in their roles for years to come.
Interoperability 
Infrastructure and 
Modernization
Helps rural communities connect and deliver medical care by upgrading local technology networks, purchasing patient and provider
devices, and leveraging group purchasing power for new solutions and infrastructure. Distributes critical hardware, software,and 
technical assistance to improve clinical efficiency and enhance cybersecurity capacity. Launches transformative technology projects to 
improve overall health outcomes for rural populations.
Population Health 
Infrastructure
Transforms care delivery across rural communities through strategic partnerships. Supports the development of innovative, community-
based care models to improve long-term population health reducing costs and preventable hospital visits. Connects local health 
stakeholders to establish highly coordinated medical interventions at the most appropriate and cost-effective site including in-home. 
Facility modifications may be permissible if in alignment with permissible use of funds for construction as defined by the program.

B.3. SNAP 
Waivers
SNAP provides food benefits to low -income families to supplement their 
grocery budget. Restricting the use of SNAP benefits on non -nutritious foods 
can help improve dietary intake and clinical indicators associated with long -
term disease in rural populations. This State Policy Action captures State 
efforts to obtain a USDA SNAP Food Restriction Waiver to prohibit 
purchase of non-nutritious items.
At baseline, WI had active 
legislation to create 
SNAP waiver
WI committed to 
this State Policy 
Action
In Progress $4.98M
C.3. Certificate 
of Need
Certificate of Need (CON) laws require healthcare providers to seek 
permission from the State before they can open new facilities or expand 
service offerings. Eliminating or loosening CON laws allows providers to 
establish new facilities in underserved rural regions without increased 
burden, addressing the scarcity of local care options. This State Policy 
Action captures State efforts to ease CON laws across several service 
and facility types.
At baseline, WI had 
limited CON regulations
No State 
commitment N/A $2.16M

Wyoming
State Snapshot: 2026 Release

Wyoming
Initiative Name Initiative Description
Access to emergency 
medical care
Shores up rural Wyoming's access to basic medical infrastructure across underserved communities. Secures essential regional resources 
for ambulance services, emergency departments, and labor and delivery units. Establishes the “Critical Access Hospital – Basic” (CAHB) 
incentive program to offer essential community services and emergency medical services regionalization efforts. 
Rural workforce supply
Grows the supply of essential healthcare workers across Wyoming using a strategic combination of individual education supportawards, 
educational capacity grants, and the 5-year required service periods. Expands training capacity at local schools while implementing 
mandatory five-year service commitments in rural areas. Establishes a highly skilled, sustainable workforce to meet the long-term clinical 
needs of remote counties through workforce education individual support, physician post-graduate medical education slots, workforce 
education start-up.
Health technology 
transformation
Leverages sensible technology investments to help groups of rural providers deliver better patient care closer to home. Expands remote 
patient access to clinical consultations and deepens the patient-provider relationship through virtual communication tools. Enhances the 
long-term management of chronic diseases while improving clinical efficiency through regional collaboration. Addresses the technology 
adoption challenge to improve care delivery closer to home, build interoperability across providers, and cut wasteful administrative 
activity. Establishes a statewide tele-specialist platform, a non-emergency transportation coordination platform, and improved 
centralized billing capacity. 
Make Wyoming Healthy 
Again
Improves the metabolic, behavioral, and cardiovascular health of rural residents through integrated primary care expansion, clinically-
integrated care coordination for chronic disease, exercise, and healthy diet promotion. Integrated primary care expansion increases the 
number and geographical reach of Federally Qualified Health Centers around Wyoming, with a focus on improving the integrationof
primary care with behavioral health, obstetric and gynecological care, as well as dental and preventive health services. Exercise and 
healthy diet promotion efforts establish partnerships with local non-profits to defray operational costs for organizing physicalevidence-
based activities.

$207.23M

B.2. Health and 
Lifestyle 
Rural populations are disproportionately impacted by chronic diseases. 
Promoting prevention through physical activity and proper nutrition can 
reduce overall cost of care burden and improve health outcomes. This State 
Policy Action captures whether a State requires schools to reestablish 
the Presidential Fitness Test.
At baseline, WY did not 
require schools to 
reestablish the 
Presidential Fitness Test
WY committed to 
this State Policy 
Action
No Progress $1.14M
B.3. SNAP 
Waivers
SNAP provides food benefits to low -income families to supplement their 
grocery budget. Restricting the use of SNAP benefits on non -nutritious foods 
can help improve dietary intake and clinical indicators associated with long -
term disease in rural populations. This State Policy Action captures State 
efforts to obtain a USDA SNAP Food Restriction Waiver to prohibit 
purchase of non-nutritious items.
At baseline, WY did not 
have a SNAP waiver in 
place

Action
Complete $6.64M
B.4. Nutrition 
Continuing 
Medical 
Education
Improved nutrition education among physicians through continuing medical 
education (CME) requirements can directly contribute to improving the 
health of Americans who live in rural areas given the disproportionate 
impact of chronic disease on rural America. This State Policy Action 
captures State efforts to require nutrition to be a component for CME.
At baseline, WY did not 
require nutrition to be 
included in CME for 
physicians

this State Policy 
Action
